## 0. Work queue (consolidated 2026-07-10; 2-pass-audited same day)

### ⚠️ How to enumerate the queue in a fresh session — READ FIRST

**A top-down read of the §0 tables alone is NOT sufficient and has already produced a wrong
"what's left" answer** (2026-07-25: the five blocked tunes, T9, F-OPT and F-SYNC were all reported
as "queue empty except D3" because none of them had a §0 row — they existed only as prose bullets
or in a sibling doc). Open items live in **seven** places (**a 7th was added 2026-08-02** — §3's
unchecked verification boxes, which had been open ~30 days with no enumeration pass naming them).
Read all seven, in this order:

| # | location | what only lives here | how to find it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **§0 / §0a tables** (this file) | programs, audit rows, weekly bug-queue mirror, **groups G and H** | `grep -n "\| TODO\|\| OWNER\|\| OPEN\|IN-PROGRESS\|BUILT-HOLD" ` on this file. ⚠️ **`\| OPEN` was ADDED 2026-08-02** — groups G and H spell an open row's status `OPEN`, not `TODO`, so the original four-alternative grep saw them only by accident (via an `OWNER` **tier** cell) and missed every `clean`/`data`-tier open row outright |
| 2 | **§4b chip register** (this file) | one-off findings parked as background tasks | `awk -F'\|' 'NF>3 && $5 ~ /^ *(\*\*)?(OPEN\|PARTIAL\|RESEARCHED)/ {print $2" => "substr($5,1,60)}'` — anchor on the status cell's **first word**: the DONE cells are long narratives that mention "OPEN" mid-sentence, so a plain `/OPEN/` returns false positives, and **a status cell must therefore LEAD with its verdict**. ⚠️ **Escaped-pipe trap (found by E2E-01 T4, 2026-07-31): a cell containing `\|` (e.g. task_f10a03's `(version\|instrument)`, G15's `\|close−open\|`) shifts the status out of field 5 — the awk MISSES a genuinely OPEN chip and can read mid-cell garbage as status.** Cross-check with a last-cell anchor (`grep -n '\| OPEN \|$'` on this file) before trusting the awk's list |
| 3 | **§9 architecture candidates** (this file) | Ousterhout deepening rows 02–06 | read §9a/§9b directly; they carry **no status column** — see the §9 rule below, a script that reads the last cell here reports DONE rows as open |
| 4 | **prose bullets inside dated UPDATE blocks** (this file) | items declared mid-narrative and never promoted to a row | `grep -n "OPEN CHIPS\|new ledger item\|stays open\|not yet built"` |
| 5 | **the newest `docs/signal-analysis/` tune table** | the whole **T-namespace** (T1…T23) — regenerated each session, no durable register of its own | `grep -n "^\| T[0-9]" docs/signal-analysis/$(ls docs/signal-analysis \| grep session-findings \| tail -1)` then keep rows whose status is `PROPOSED` |
| 6 | **the current weekly bug queue** (`docs/signal-analysis/YYYY-MM-DD-weekly-bug-queue.md`) | B/S/D rows + the "blocked tunes" and "parked proposal" bullets under the owner-decision pack | read the whole file — the closing bullets carry items the table does not |
| 7 | **§3 "Verification only — next market session"** (this file) — **ADDED 2026-08-02, it was the SEVENTH location all along** | unchecked `- [ ]` verification boxes, incl. live **gate** inputs (#512's `sentimentPct` vs `sentimentLevelPct` compare) | `grep -n "^- \[ \]" ` on this file. Three boxes went unchecked from 2026-07-03 to at least 2026-08-02 without any enumeration pass naming them, because the recipe said "six" and this section was not one of them (shard C Part 1 #5 → row H5) |

**Single-status rule.** An item's status is authoritative in **this file's** row and nowhere else.
Source docs (bug queues, session findings, audits) carry evidence and may show a status for local
readability, but **when they disagree, the ledger row wins** — and the drifted one gets corrected in
the same PR. Two live examples of the drift this rule exists to stop: `task_a2ae20ed` read OPEN here
while its own sweep doc said CLOSED; bug-queue rows B1–B4 read `IN PROGRESS`/`queued` for a day after
all four had merged AND deployed.

**Promotion rule.** Anything declared open in a prose bullet, a tune table, or a sibling doc **must
get a §0 row in the same PR that declares it.** A bullet is not a queue entry — nobody finds it.
⚠️ **Corollary the promotion rule does NOT cover, and it has bitten: promoting an item does not
retire the prose that spawned it.** The paragraph keeps its open-item wording forever, because nobody
goes back to edit narrative once a row exists. **When an item is promoted, leave a `→ row Gn` pointer
on the prose in the SAME edit** (see the F-OPT/F-SYNC paragraphs in §0's 2026-07-20 block for the
shape). Before briefing anything sourced from prose, grep the item name for a promoted row.

**⚠️ §9 rows carry NO status column — strikethrough on the id is the DONE marker.** Their columns are
`# | deepening | badge | files | gate`, so the LAST cell is *"how you would take it"* and legitimately
reads e.g. `**HOLD-tier parity firewall …**` on a row that shipped weeks ago. The status lives (a) as
`~~**04**~~` strikethrough on the id and (b) inside the *deepening* cell, which leads with `DONE`.
**A struck-through id means DONE in every table in this file and overrides every other signal.**

⚠️ **COST RECORD 2026-08-02 — one enumeration pass produced FOUR false dispatches, and the ledger was
only half the cause.** Two (F-OPT, F-SYNC) came from the prose corollary above. Two (§9-02, §9-04)
came from a parser that read §9's last cell as a status and saw `HOLD-tier …` — both rows were struck
through and said `DONE 2026-07-29` in cell 2. Every builder caught its own premise before writing
code, which is the only reason this cost minutes. **Two takeaways: a scripted enumeration must
implement the strikethrough rule and the six locations, not just grep for status words; and every
brief must carry a STEP 0 telling the builder to verify the premise against the code first.**

**THE THREE FINDINGS THAT MUST SURVIVE — read these before trusting anything downstream of them.**

1. ⚠️ **#941's `SessionLivenessHeartbeat` was UNARMED for 14 days and read as armed, because the
   SIBLING batch var is set.** The bean is `@ConditionalOnProperty(name = "artha.heartbeat.session-url")`
   (`SessionLivenessHeartbeat.java:62`), fed by `ARTHA_HEARTBEAT_SESSION_URL`
   (`deploy/docker-compose.yml:620`); the sibling `SwingBatchHeartbeat` keys on
   `artha.heartbeat.url` / `ARTHA_HEARTBEAT_URL`. #941 merged 2026-07-19; the session var was EMPTY
   until the owner supplied a URL on 2026-08-02, so the only detector for a stack that dies
   *mid-session* was dormant the whole time while the once-a-day 20:15 batch pinger kept reporting
   green. **Status is `ARMED-UNVERIFIED`, not armed.** The URL was appended to `.env` in place (ACL
   verified unchanged) and strategy-signal recreated; `ARTHA_HEARTBEAT_SESSION_URL` is now SET in the
   container (len 56, value not printed). **Bean construction could NOT be confirmed:** actuator
   exposure is `health,info,prometheus` only (`application.yml:289`) so `/actuator/beans` 404s, and the
   class logs nothing at construction — its only log sites are inside `pingIfHealthy` (`:119`, `:121`).
   The KNOWN-ARMED sibling is equally silent, **so silence proves nothing in either direction.** A ping
   fires only inside a live NSE session (cron `0 */10 9-15 * * MON-FRI` + `MarketCalendar.isOpen` +
   the narrowed session window), so the first provable evidence is Monday. Scheduled task
   `verify-session-heartbeat-armed-20260803` checks it 2026-08-03 10:00 IST.
2. ⚠️ **T1 / T7 / G13 / G10 are CONDITIONAL, not FINAL** (#1235, chip `task_2735acfb`). "Measured under
   the 30-minute `time_stop`" was a **harness** parameter — `ExitKnobs.timeStopBars`, counted in

a third deploy round rebuilt strategy-signal, market-data, backtest, edge-gateway and frontend-react
from `aa610b04`. The staleness is kept on the record below because **how it was found is reusable and
the fact that it is now fixed is not.**

Three deploy rounds ran: `bf93ebf7` (flyway-init force-recreated → **V055 applied**, probed by name in
`pg_constraint` AND by a rejected insert; jar fingerprints confirmed #1223/#1225/#1226) · `70911904`
(**M40**) · and the final `aa610b04` full round at ~19:37 IST.

| service | image built (IST) | state @ ~19:40 IST |
|---|---|---|
| strategy-signal | 2026-08-02 19:37 | **CURRENT** — was stale by #1234 |
| market-data | 2026-08-02 19:37 | **CURRENT** — was stale by #1234 |
| backtest | 2026-08-02 19:37 | **CURRENT** — was stale by #1234 + #1197 |
| edge-gateway | 2026-08-02 19:37 | **CURRENT** — was stale by #1234 + `cec369ab` |
| frontend-react | 2026-08-02 19:36 | **CURRENT** — was the materially stale one at 4 runtime commits: #1212 (`Insight.priority` type-bug fix), #1193 (F5 U3 data-health flags), #1169 (G16 near-miss dot probe), #1222 (`contracts.bridge.ts`). A 5th commit, #1199 @ `db726c3e`, touched only `package.json`'s `gen:api` script — build tooling, not runtime, and correctly not counted |
| optimizer-service | 2026-08-01 03:55 | **CURRENT** — 0 runtime-source commits since its image |
| margin-service | 2026-07-19 18:35 | **CURRENT** — 0 runtime-source commits since its image |

**⭐ THE PROBE IS THE POINT — it FAILED, then PASSED, on the same command.** The drift was found by
asking the running service what it serves, not by comparing timestamps:

```
committed  BacktestTradeItem.entryPrice → {"type": "string"}
live @ ~19:20  →  {"type": "number"}    ← FAILED: spec-generation drift, four Java services stale
live @ ~19:40  →  {"type": "string"}    ← PASSES after the aa610b04 round
```

(in-container `:8083/v3/api-docs`.) An image-build timestamp can only say a service *might* be stale;
this says what it *actually serves*. **Runtime money behaviour was never affected** — the drift was
purely in the generated spec — but the live `/v3/api-docs` genuinely disagreed with the committed
contract for those hours, and nothing but this probe would have shown it.

⚠️ **NESTED-JAR FINGERPRINT TRAP, hit again for real on this very deploy — it cost a FALSE NEGATIVE.**
Confirming #1234 means finding `BigDecimalStringModelConverter` + `BigDecimalStringCustomizer`, which
live in **`common-web-core`**, a nested lib. Measured on all four Java services:

```
unzip -l /app/*.jar | grep -c BigDecimalString                    → 0     ← the trap
extract BOOT-INF/lib/common-web-core-*.jar first, then grep      → 3     ← the truth
```

The 3 are `BigDecimalStringCustomizer.class`, `BigDecimalStringModelConverter.class` and its nested
`BigDecimalStringModelConverter$Decimal.class` (a record), all stamped `19:33`. **A 0 from the outer
fat jar is not evidence of absence** — nested libs must be extracted first. Same 4-for-4 on
strategy-signal / market-data / backtest / edge-gateway.

**Also re-verified AFTER the recreate, because a redeploy is exactly when a previously-proven thing
can silently regress:** M40's two classes still present in the strategy-signal jar; **V055 still
enforcing** (`pg_constraint` count 1); frontend-react serving bundle **`index-D0wPA9GI.js`, the same
hash as the `dist/` built at 19:33** — an identity check, not an mtime comparison.

⚠️ **`installed=f` on the newest engine-reload row is NORMAL and must not be read as a failed
install.** Reloads come in PAIRS after every deploy and the flag alternates — measured across all
three of today's rounds, ~38 s apart each time:

| reload id | loaded / unresolved / load_errors | `installed` | IST |
|---|---|---|---|
| 97 → 98 | 38 / 0 / 0 | `t` → `f` | 16:54 → 16:55 |
| 99 → 100 | 38 / 0 / 0 | `t` → `f` | 18:45 → 18:46 |
| **101 → 102** | **38 / 0 / 0** | **`t` → `f`** | **19:38 → 19:39** |

The first of each pair **installs**; the second is the **reconcile finding no drift**, so it
legitimately installs nothing. Read `loaded / unresolved / load_errors` for health — **38 / 0 / 0**
on the current row — and per the standing rule, health is `unresolved == 0`, never `loaded > 0`.
This was briefly misread as a failed install at closeout, which is why it is written down here.

design asks the DB, never the engine: bars-present + rejections-absent, K=7 bars, in-process JDBC, bounded
`forceResubscribe` heal.

**⚠️ THE BRIEF GATE CAUGHT ITS OWN AUTHOR ~20 MIN AFTER SHIPPING.** The owner-approved unified-detector design
(`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-16-engine-liveness-detector.md`) is **INVALIDATED at §3.1** — its core premise
is FALSE, proven live 2026-07-17: **REST cannot answer "are bars flowing"** (queried at 03:46 IST, market shut,
zero bars → `{"status":"GREEN","marketOpen":false,"tickedTokens":25,"problems":[]}` — `tickedTokens` is a
process-LIFETIME map size, `DataHealthState:25`, and `TICK_BAR_DIVERGENCE` skips stale-tick tokens,
`DataHealthCanary:136`); and **JDBC cannot either** — `SET ROLE ay_strategy; SELECT ... marketdata.candles` →
`permission denied for schema marketdata` (`admin/V001:18`). The doc carries a STOP block. ~~§1/§2 (the 9-of-9
measurement + why every existing detector is blind) are unaffected and REMAIN authoritative.~~ Reviving it needs
an OWNER decision: a market-data bounded-recent-closed-bar contract, OR a cross-schema grant + an ADR.

**⚠️ UPDATE 2026-07-17 — §1 IS NOW INVALIDATED TOO. THE DETECTOR'S CORE PREMISE IS DEAD ON A SECOND,
INDEPENDENT AXIS. Read this before reviving F10 Part B.** "Bars present + rejections absent ⇒ the engine is
broken" is **FALSE**: it would **false-positive on every bearish leg**. Proven by two independent investigations
(Opus + Fable, separate models, no shared framing) plus a live thread dump, after the Architect escalated an
84-minute silence as a live starvation — **and was wrong; the engine was healthy the whole time.**
**MECHANISM:** `SignalEngine.evaluateAtBarClose:1003-1013` has **no else-branch** — both `recordRejection` call
sites sit INSIDE `evaluation.isPresent() && evaluation.get().entry()`, so `strategy.signal_rejections` records
**only CONFLUENCE-gate blocks on bars whose CHART gate already passed**. A chart-stage no-entry writes nothing.
Every published scalper shares the same two required scorers on one 3m NIFTY-future series (`rsi14`/`rsi_momentum`
w=1.0 + `supertrend`/`direction` w=1.0, `threshold: 0.2`), so composite = (s_rsi + s_st)/2 ⇒ **SuperTrend DOWN +
RSI < 58 ⇒ composite < 0.2 ⇒ EVERY scalper silent simultaneously**. **The "9 rejections per 3m" cadence is NOT a
health baseline — it is a supertrend-UP indicator.** Both agents reconstructed ST(10,2)+RSI(14) from live data and
predicted **every gap edge bar-exactly on two separate days**, including an isolated single-bar hole no stall theory
can produce; Fable additionally matched a **partial** batch gate-by-gate (the 5 CE gated `close > vwma20` silent,
the 4 vwap-only CE wrote, the 2 PE gated `close < vwma20` wrote) and observed that `scalp-trending-oi-nifty`
(`close > vwma20`) **and its PE twin** (`close < vwma20`) were BOTH silent for 80 min — exactly one gate can be true
per bar, so only the shared composite can silence both. **A thread dump taken DURING a blackout shows `signal-eval`
parked on `LinkedBlockingQueue.take()` — its own executor queue, `cpu=1724ms / elapsed=6574s` — an IDLE worker, not
a stall.** So §1's "zero/truncated rejections on 9 of 9 trading days" is plausibly **measuring market direction, not
engine health** (that figure was never re-derived — it is `recalled`). What survives: **every existing detector is
still blind** (§2), and the silent no-entry path was a REAL observability defect — 4 states (engine dead / chart-gate
closed / indicators warming / composite below threshold) shared ONE DB signature. **FIXED + DEPLOYED LIVE:
[#895](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/895) @ 17a69ea2** — `ay_signal_eval_outcome_total{outcome}`,
7 tags, **verified live pre-registered at 0.0 on a fresh boot** (a lazily-created counter would leave a MISSING `fired`
series indistinguishable from a dead engine — the same ambiguity, one level up). Built **structurally**: `decideEntry`
now RETURNS an `Outcome` and `evaluateAtBarClose` holds the ONLY increment site, so **a future silent `return;` is a
COMPILE ERROR, not a blind spot**. The `Σ(outcomes) == evaluations` invariant is mutation-proven BOTH ways (undercount
34 RED, double-count 80 RED, real 40 GREEN) — **`>= 40` would have gone green at 80**, hence an exact assertion.
⚠️ **RETRACTED 2026-07-18 — an earlier version of THIS BLOCK claimed #895 "gives a future F10 Part B revival a sound
liveness primitive it lacked". THAT CLAIM IS FALSE and was the Architect's, written the same day, unverified.**
`ay_signal_eval_outcome_total`'s increment site sits INSIDE the `else` of `if (activeEntry.isPresent())`
(`SignalEngine:1063`, counter at `:1105-1113`), BELOW two `continue`s — `!inUniverse` (`:1017`) and
`!withinSessionWindow` (`:1020-1022`) — and btst is never evaluated from `onClosedBar` at all (`:1026-1029`).
**So Σ(outcomes) is LEGITIMATELY FLAT while bars flow** in four normal states: outside a session window, whole book in
position, context-only symbols, btst. **Measured live: 51 of the intraday strategies close their window at
15:00/12:00/14:30 while the watchdog arms 09:20→15:30 (`SubscriberHealthCanary:72-73`) — so Σ(outcomes) is
STRUCTURALLY FLAT 15:00–15:30 EVERY non-expiry day.** A "bars arriving + Σ flat ⇒ alarm" detector **pages every
afternoon**. It is **the same mistake as the dead design, one layer up**: rejections were *direction*-dependent;
Σ(outcomes) is *window*- and *position*-dependent. **#895 is an ATTRIBUTION primitive — an evidence panel. NEVER arm
anything on it.**
**THE ONLY signals safe to key liveness on** are `lastBarReceivedAtMs` (`SignalEngine:361`, stamped at `:954` as the
FIRST line of `onCandleMessage` — before ANY universe/window/position/loaded logic) and `lastBarEvaluatedAtMs`. Both
are direction-, window- AND position-independent. Rejections / `signals` / gate output / Σ(outcomes) are all confounded.
**PART B IS ALREADY SHIPPED — IT IS #886, AND THE OWNER'S FORK IS MOOT.** Post-#886 `SubscriberHealthCanary`: gate 3 is
`registry.countEnabledPublished() == 0` (`:125`; live = 69) so `loaded == 0` no longer disarms it (pinned by
`SubscriberHealthCanaryTest:119`); gate 4 is `feedAge != Long.MAX_VALUE && feedAge > feedFreshMs` (`:172`) so
"can't tell" is suspicious not healthy; knobs wired (`application.yml:63-66`, `docker-compose.yml:393-395`).
**F10 replay against the live code: boot ~08:45 → `loaded=0` → no subscriptions → no bars; `lastBarReceivedAtMs` seeded
at boot (`:424`); at the 09:20 sweep `receiveGap ≈ 35min ≫ 180s`, `evalLag ≈ 0`, feed fresh → IT FIRES.** The
cold-start is detected today. **And the market-data-contract-vs-cross-schema-grant fork is moot on BOTH prongs** — the
engine already holds `lastBarReceivedAtMs`, a strictly BETTER oracle than either: a market-data contract answers "did
market-data WRITE bars", whose YES does not imply the engine RECEIVED them (the 07-07 subscription drop is exactly that
gap), and the grant punctures D10 for a worse answer. **Build neither.**
**⚠️ ~~DO NOT ARM chip task_a6c12601 / #868 `SignalStarvationCanary` — it is a LIVE LANDMINE (→ chip task_3a928626,
retire the class).~~ RESOLVED 2026-07-26 — THE CLASS IS RETIRED, not left dormant** (see the task_3a928626 row in §4b).
The reasoning below is kept because it is the reusable lesson, not because the class still exists.** It keys on `lastGateOutputAtMs`, written only PAST the chart gate, so on a SuperTrend-DOWN leg
`outputGap > windowMs` while its guards (`receiveGap < barGapMs`, `evalLag < barGapMs`) are BOTH satisfied ⇒ **it
pages**. **The 2026-07-17 false alarm was this canary's exact signature, executed by hand.** Arming it automates that
mistake daily.
**The one GENUINE gap:** `SwingBatchHeartbeat` (`:36`, cron `0 15 20 * * MON-FRI` at `:51`) pings ONCE at 20:15 IST and
proves only that the swing BATCH ran — **a stack dead 09:15–15:30 that recovers by 20:15 pings happily while the whole
session is lost.** A session-cadence heartbeat (~10min, 09:30–15:20 IST, gated ONLY on `receiveGap < barGapMs`, never on
outcomes) on the existing external dead-man's-switch closes it; absence-is-the-alarm survives process death and cannot
be muted in-process. Owner-gated on creating the external check.
**BUILT + MERGED 2026-07-19 — [#941](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/941) @ f1b412f6 (`SessionLivenessHeartbeat`, strategy-signal; clean-tier, default-OFF, UNDEPLOYED — dormant, rides the next deploy).** The IN-STACK half is closed: `@Scheduled(0 */10 9-15, Asia/Kolkata)` pings a SEPARATE external monitor every ~10min within [09:30,15:20) IST, gated ONLY on `SignalEngine.lastBarReceivedAtMs()` receive-gap (< `bar-gap-ms`) + calendar-open — never rejections/gate-output/outcomes (the SignalStarvationCanary false-page class). Withholds the ping when bars aren't arriving (absence-is-the-alarm). Default pool, NOT `monitorTaskScheduler` (a 5s HTTP ping there would starve `SubscriberHealthCanary.sweep`). Dormant via `@ConditionalOnProperty(artha.heartbeat.session-url)` + `artha.signals.engine-enabled` (codex cross-vendor caught the missing engine-lifecycle condition = an engine-off boot break the builder+Architect both missed). **OWNER STEP TO ARM:** create the external check (healthchecks.io/UptimeRobot) expecting a ~10min ping in-window, set `ARTHA_HEARTBEAT_SESSION_URL` in `.env`, redeploy. ⚠️ arming caveat: a weekday NSE holiday withholds ALL pings (correct — no session) → pause the check on NSE holidays or use a generous grace, else a holiday false-alarm.

Build history: Owner funded "finish it (M1+M2+tests+review)" 2026-07-27; same-day close-out: main-merge reconciliation (detector machinery deduped, migrations renumbered **V049–V051** after V047 detector + V048 exit-reason, swing EXITs persist their reason), the round-4 **TOCTOU Critical** fixed by per-anchor `pg_advisory_xact_lock` serialization (exit discovery inside the effect tx; `PaperService.openOrder` refuses an EXPIRED anchor), the mutation-verified regression suite COMMITTED (it had existed only untracked while its receipt was cited — round-4 Major), and the detector page made arming-aware (round-5 Major: it recommended a manual `/run` that would duplicate a queued replay). **Cross-vendor: APPROVED round 6, PROMOTION_READY, no findings deferred** (7 Criticals + 9 Majors closed across the branch's lifetime). Full reactor 1106 tests (one `SignalEngineIntegrationTest` counter flake under double-load, green on isolated rerun). Ships default-OFF (`artha.swing.catchup-enabled`); deploy needs flyway force-recreate + 3 table probes; arming is a separate later owner decision. Historical record of the split + the six deferred Criticals follows (all now fixed): ✅ **SPLIT 2026-07-26 (owner-approved): DETECT ships, REPLAY parks.** Four review rounds established that the branch divides cleanly along *knowing* vs *doing*: every one of the 7 Criticals lives in the auto-replay half (as-of lot filtering, atomic claim-vs-money-effect, partial-session sweep, two-fetch readiness), and **zero** live in the detection half. So the incident that motivated the whole branch — nobody knew the 07-17 batch had not run for 3 days — is closed by paging a human, without shipping a machine that re-trades a missed session unattended. Three outcomes: **(a)** the detector SHIPPED as **[#1044](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1044) @ `f64f8f07`, DEPLOYED + ARMED live 2026-07-26** (`ARTHA_SIGNALS_SWING_MISSED_BATCH_DETECTOR_ENABLED=true` — it replaces an always-on canary, so leaving it default-false would have been a net coverage regression; V047 applied 046→047 with flyway-init forced, both tables DB-probed, `swing-detector-sched-1` thread confirmed live with `SwingBatchCanary.check()` its only task, engine 38 strategies `unresolved=0` after restart). **Behaviour is NOT yet verified** — the schedulers fire Mon–Fri 20:00/20:05, so the intent table was empty at deploy and the first sweep with real data is the morning after the first weekday evening; a recurring 08:40 IST weekday check is scheduled, and Monday's expected result is TOTAL SILENCE against an empty table (a page there would be a false positive and a real bug). **Four cross-vendor review rounds closed 2 Criticals + 3 Majors, none reachable by the test suite:** the first cut was BLIND to the container-down case (candidates came only from intent rows, which only the schedulers write — so a session where the JVM was down at cron had no row and could never page, a net regression vs the canary it deletes); the terminal latch was taken on a fire-and-forget `@Async` publish that swallows exceptions, so "paged" meant "enqueued"; the fix for THAT then created an alarm with no exit, because a past session can never acquire a run marker (`SwingBatchRecorder` stamps today), so "repeat until it runs" would have paged ~64 times per incident — capped at 5; the calendar cliff silently disabled the container-down sweep with no log; and the migration still asserted the removed falsehood in a `COMMENT ON TABLE`, which lands in the live catalog. Follow-up orphan closed as [#1046](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1046) (chip task_bc04c4d3). — a single V047 records *schedule-time* arming intent so a session can no longer be mistaken for deliberately-disarmed (the old canary read CURRENT `@Value` flags + a last-run watermark, Critical 5's root cause), V047 latches one page per session, dedicated scheduler, **never invokes a batch or touches a paper position**; **(b)** M2's emergency-close defect was extracted and **MERGED as [#1043](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1043)** (`4ca5485`) — it was never swing-related, just noticed there; **(c)** the auto-replay half is parked as **draft [#1036](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1036)** and owes permanent tests for the round-4 fixes plus an unreviewed round 4 before it can be reconsidered. Reviving it is an owner decision, not a queue item. Historical detail below. ⛔ **DEFERRED 2026-07-26, NOT SHIPPABLE: 6 Critical money defects + 2 Major regressions** found by a fresh cross-vendor review on the CURRENT base. Rebase + renumber are DONE and pushed as **`origin/feat/swing-batch-catchup`** (use this, not the old `worktree-agent-abb02bf43adbb895d`): zero-conflict rebase onto main, `V046__swing_catchup_runs`→**`V047`** (main's V046__engine_reloads is applied live; lineage V044..V047 contiguous), verify green **1019 tests**. THE SIX CRITICALS: (1) the sweep CONTINUES past a partial session — Thursday's bar missing + Friday's present ⇒ Friday closes the position at Friday's price and Thursday's retry then sees no anchor and falsely completes; (2) historical runs evaluate CURRENTLY-active lots without filtering as-of the pinned bar date, so a Friday anchor settles against Thursday's truncated bar and Manas can close an averaged position containing FUTURE lots; (3) durable `PENDING` rows fall out of the `maxAttempts+2` window forever — a 7-session outage leaves the oldest session never reaching DONE or ABANDONED; (4) the claim is NOT atomic with the money effect — paper failures are swallowed in the listeners while the sweep still declares DONE, leaving an active signal with no paper open or a position with no anchor; (5) `DISARMED` records CURRENT intent, so re-enabling replays a deliberately-skipped session and disabling suppresses an armed-but-missed one; (6) readiness and candidates come from two SEPARATE HTTP fetches, so it can validate session X and trade session Y's names. MAJORS: the unqualified `@Scheduled` shares the default pool with the 15s SL/TP sweep, funnel calls have no timeout and the market-hours check is START-ONLY (the Architect reached this one independently); and the manual-close path now runs full `positionDetail()` enrichment BEFORE closing, so an emergency explicit-price close can hang. ⚠️ **Findings 1/2/3/5 look BASE-INDEPENDENT — i.e. missed by the three cross-vendor rounds this branch passed in July, not introduced by the rebase. Treat any revived long-parked branch as unreviewed.** Owner decision: fix the six, or solve the missed-batch incident another way. ⚠️ **MIGRATION NUMBER NOW COLLIDES (verified 2026-07-25):** the branch carries `V046__swing_catchup_runs.sql`, but main shipped `V046__engine_reloads.sql` and it is APPLIED LIVE (`strategy.flyway_schema_history`, 2026-07-25 09:18 UTC). Reviving this branch **must renumber its migration to `V047`** — never `outOfOrder=true`, and never re-use 046, or flyway-init fails *validation* and blocks every future migration. (The old note "V046 unmerged so main stays deployable" is stale — main is deployable because 046 is engine_reloads, not because the number is free.) This is the ONLY non-`main` branch left on the remote after the 2026-07-25 cleanup; every other branch was verified shipped and deleted. Gives the Manas/Minervini swing batch a catch-up path (the gap that cost `paper_positions id=29` OMAXAUTO a missed stop after the 07-17 stack outage — ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-03: this cited `id=28`, which is SBCL (a manas-arora SELL closed 07-17); OMAXAUTO is `id=29`. "3 days" was calendar days, not sessions — only ONE session closed below the 229.9553 stop (07-17 @ 228.38); 07-20 closed back above at 233.72, so a batch running that evening would have said HOLD**). Canary-window (08:35 IST) default-OFF trigger, atomic durable claim (V046 `swing_catchup_runs` + `DISARMED` status) + in-process mutex, oldest-first bounded sweep. **5 real findings across 3 review rounds, ALL closed** (double-fill via `PaperService` averaging; partial-run-stamps-complete; no-retry; re-arm-replays-disarmed-sessions; failed-marker-becomes-DONE) — 991 tests, the double-fill IT asserts ONE position not two, Golden/Parity 9/9. Owner-approved "merge+arm" but the Architect HELD the arm (won't arm a money path that found a bug every round without a clean final pass). **Arms nothing tomorrow regardless — tonight's 20:00 batch ran clean, no session to catch up.** OMAXAUTO itself was manually closed live at the 07-17 NSE daily close 228.38 (−₹1,613.71, `MANUAL`), and the `POST /positions/{id}/close` no-audit-row gap is fixed inside this branch.

Correct by design: 07-17 predates the intent ledger (`swing_batch_schedule_intents` `count=2, min=max=2026-07-27`), so schedule-time arming is unknowable and the code refuses rather than inferring today's flag — exactly the round-5 Critical's requirement. **Money safety: paper positions opened 0 / closed 0, orders 0, paper_events 0 over the 12 h spanning the sweep.** `ABANDONED` is terminal (`retryableSessions` selects only PENDING/stale-RUNNING) and 07-17 leaves the window on 07-29, so no repeat. **Generalizable lesson: the first armed sweeps of any as-of replay reach back before its own intent ledger exists — expect one fail-closed terminal row per genuinely-missing pre-ledger session in the window, and do not read those rows as a fault.** The 07-29 sweep should be the true silent no-op. **Still unobserved: a successful replay to `DONE`** (needs an armed session that actually misses); today exercised only the refusal branch. Residual, not fixed and not worth fixing: the refusal alerts an operator about a session nothing can act on — one-time noise by construction.

**RESOLVED — investigation, NOT a bug:** the batch-verify agent's "NFO option 1m capture DEAD (0 rows today)" RED was a **misread** (and the Architect's own subscription-death hypothesis was ALSO wrong). "0 today" is EXPECTED — the current 21JUL week hasn't expired. Nothing to fix. If LIVE intraday option premium is ever wanted, it's a NEW `OptionAtmPinner` feature (~160 tokens « 3000 cap), not a repair — **ledger item F-OPT below**, not a chip.

| C8 | `int-i4-arming` | INT I4 — delivery arming + calibration | INT §12 I4 | owner | **DELIVERY HALF DONE + ARMED LIVE 2026-08-01** — owner un-parked it with "Arm both" (2026-08-01 pre-sleep); [#1040](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1040) merged @ 345c202a after **4 cross-vendor review rounds fixed 6 pre-arm Majors** (insert-vs-refresh publish gating via `Upsert(insight, inserted)`; mute exempt from the BLOCKED trust gate; status-blind `isCooling`; transport retry split from audit persistence; `delivery:true` mute markers; then a three-way delivery decision — INSERT / severity-escalation vs the latest ANY-status prior via `findLatest` (an OPEN-only compare silently suppressed ACKed-WARN→CRITICAL) / cooldown-cycle expiry — all four cases pinned + red-proofed, 35/35 green). Armed in `.env` (in place, ACL intact): ntfy + WS at the NOTICE floor, Telegram off; container recreated, env verified in-container, jar fingerprint carries `findLatest`, engine 38 loaded / 0 dropped. Expected live rate: ~1 push/family/day for a persistent condition + 1 per genuine escalation; CONTEXT_SHIFT paces at ~45 min. First-armed-day probe: ACK a live WARN → confirm silence; observe an escalation → confirm the page. **CALIBRATION HALF still evidence-gated** (needs 4+ weeks of quality reports; re-measure the true delivery rate on live data before any floor change). Superseded history: **PARKED until ~2026-08-09 (owner, 2026-07-26).** Delivery half BUILT at the NOTICE floor ([#1040](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1040), converted to DRAFT) but **NOT ARMED** — cross-vendor review returned **7 Majors**. ⚠️ **The headline invalidates a number that was reported to the owner twice:** the "3.57 pushes/day" dry-run counted ROWS, not DELIVERIES. `DataTrustGenerator` uses a per-family/day dedupe key with NO cooldown, `InsightSweeper` runs every 15 min, `InsightRepository`'s upsert returns the existing row id without distinguishing insert from refresh, and `InsightEngine` republishes on every refresh — so ONE persistent CRITICAL condition delivers **~96 pushes/day while showing as a single in-app row**. The build's independent dry-run made the SAME mistake, which is why the two agreed and looked corroborated; agreement between two identical errors is not evidence. **Three Majors compound and gate arming:** (2) republish-on-refresh, (4) ACK/DISMISS bypasses cooldown because the check is OPEN-only, and (5) the noisiest alert CANNOT be muted — the controller rejects all actions when data trust is BLOCKED before reaching MUTE, and DATA_TRUST CRITICALs are themselves BLOCKED. Also open: partial-success double-push, legacy pre-I4 mutes silently becoming GLOBAL, one-way mute in the UI with no expiry. **What holds:** INFO fails closed on null/malformed/unknown severity, shipped defaults deliver nothing, all four env names byte-identical across yml/compose/.env.example, suppressed/cooling/muted rows still persist in-app, Modulith clean (a real `insights`→`notifier` cycle was found and the event record moved into `signals`), delivery async off the insight scheduler. **The calibration half was ALWAYS blocked** — the design gates it on "4+ weeks of quality reports" and there are 14 days. On revisit: fix 2/4/5 first, then RE-MEASURE the true delivery rate before any floor decision. | **DELIVERY DONE+ARMED [#1040](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1040) @ 345c202a · calibration evidence-gated (~4 wks)** |

Live-verified post-deploy: the list item emits all 14 keys in the record's declared order WITH `notificationChannel: null` present, not omitted. Order: most sources were `LinkedHashMap` (preserved); `archive` + the `list` envelope were MULTI-key `Map.of` (**NORMALISED, not preserved** — never call these byte-identical); the `versions` envelope was SINGLE-key (trivially stable). ⚠️ **Two review Majors, both in the CLAIMS not the runtime, both worth keeping:** (a) I set the ratchet to **6** from a shell grep over all of `src/main`, but the test counts only `*Controller.java` — the true count is **4**, and since the assertion is `isLessThanOrEqualTo` the wrong number PASSED GREEN while leaving room for two new opaque handlers. Count with the test's own regex; red-proof the floor. (b) `ConfigDiff.Op` published a **contract lie** — `before` is null on an add, `after` on a remove, but the generated schema declared both always-present. **Typing an opaque Map does not merely reveal a shape, it COMMITS to it**, so every nullable operand must be checked at conversion time. market-data 26→**16** ([#1131](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1131) @ `870bacc1` — every SINGLE-handler controller: `WorldIndices`, `UpstoxEntitlement`, `FuturesPreOpen`, `ContinuousFuturesAdmin`, `OiBuzz`, `Announcement`, `IvAnalytics`, `Subscriptions`, `Screener`, `OptionsChain.history`). All ten UNCONDITIONAL, and that is provable rather than assumed: nine used `Map.of`, which **throws NPE on a null value**, so every key was already non-null on any response that returned at all. **TWO needed no new record** — `UpstoxAnalyticsClient.Entitlement` and `FuturesPreOpen` already carried the same components, by name and type, in the same sequence the `Map.of` listed them. `Announcement` is the trap and is deliberately NOT `AnnouncementService.Feed`: the wire form differs on two components (`from`/`to` are the `LocalDate.toString()` STRING, and a null `symbol` is emitted as `\"\"`), so returning `Feed` would have silently changed two values. One behaviour delta, in an already-broken path only: a null component `Map.of` would have rejected with an NPE (a 500) now serialises as null. ⚠️ **THE LESSON FROM THIS SLICE — typing an ENVELOPE pulls its ITEM schema into the spec for the first time.** Those item records had never been enumerated (the Map made them `array of object`), so none declared nullability, and FIVE would have published genuinely nullable fields as NON-NULLABLE — a lie in the generated TypeScript. Caught by cross-vendor review, then caught AGAIN when my first fix was still under-scoped. Final non-null sets, each traced to its construction site: `WorldIndex`→`key` ALONE (12 nullable — `isGlobalIndex` admits a row on `instrumentKey != null && segment == GLOBAL_INDEX` and forwards every other master field unchanged, and `UpstoxGlobalInstrumentWireContractTest:53` pins a row carrying only segment+name+instrument_key as an ACCEPTED wire shape); `PreOpenRow`→`symbol`; `ScreenerService.Row`→`exchange,tradingsymbol,latestClose,pastClose,value`; `Announcement`→`date,time,symbol`; `SnapshotRow`→ exactly the V006 DDL's six NOT NULL columns. Spelled `types={\"X\",\"null\"}` (`nullable=true` is a SILENT NO-OP at 3.1) and VERIFIED in the recaptured spec. ⚠️ Third correction from the same review: **`required` is NOT the defect and must stay** — a record component is always PRESENT; what was wrong was the TYPE. The fix is the 3.1 union, never dropping `required`. market-data 16→12 ([#1134](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1134) @ `e580d207` — PreOpen + MarketSurface; `holidays()` items were themselves anonymous Maps, so a holiday row published as a bare object; both newly-exposed item records audited BEFORE typing their envelopes). market-data 12→**6** ([#1138](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1138) @ `5cc45bad` — all six FuturesAnalytics handlers, built by Codex, audited past the receipt). ⚠️ #1138 carries a PLATFORM fix, not slice-local: **`NullableRefCustomizer.appendNullEnumMember`** in common-web-core — under JSON Schema 2020-12 `type` and `enum` BOTH apply, so `type:[\"string\",\"null\"]` beside a 4-member enum REJECTS null; swagger-core never emits the null member for a Java enum, so the customizer appends it at assembly (response-only, 4 tests, red-proofed; blast radius MEASURED across all four specs = 3 lines, all market-data — the platform's only nullable enums). Also: `BankAnalysisCell` nullable at the RECORD level is the correct seam for nullable ARRAY ELEMENTS — `@ArraySchema(types=[object,null])` DESTROYS the $ref and was tried+rejected. backtest 7→**2** ([#1139](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1139) @ `e2fc9188` — Results trades+summary, Jobs list+detail, Indicators series; built by Codex, which CORRECTED the brief: `OiAttributionController.attribution` is ALSO conditional — populated = the empty 10 keys PLUS `runId`+`oiDerived`, a strict superset, so a record would force those present-as-null on the empty path). **Remaining: 12 total = 8 CONVERTIBLE (OptionsAnalytics 4: `oiAnalysis`/`strikeSeries`/`multipleOi`/`optionsChart` · strategy-signal 4: `RiskController` 2, `UniverseController` 1, `NotifierController` 1 — unblocked now #1137 merged) + 4 ASSESSED STOPS (`oiExpiry` 3-vs-4 keys, `openHighStrategy` 3-vs-5, `HeroZeroPremium` 5-vs-16, `OiAttribution` 10-vs-12 — all the HeroZeroPremium class; a record would ADD null keys to the empty response, a wire change needing a shape decision). The TERMINAL floor is 4, not 0 — when the 8 land, D3's Map-return slice is DONE and the 4 stops are the documented end state.** ✅ **MAP-RETURN SLICE CLOSED 2026-08-01 at exactly the predicted terminal floor of 4.** The last 8 landed in three PRs: backtest's two `ResponseEntity`-wrapped returns ([#1188](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1188) @ `3abed95f`), strategy-signal's last four ([#1191](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1191) @ `081d7769`), market-data's last five ([#1190](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1190) @ `4bad0c38`). Final `FROZEN` map, recounted with the test's OWN regex rather than a shell grep: **edge-gateway 0 · market-data 2 · strategy-signal 0 · backtest 2** — and those 4 are exactly the 4 assessed stops (market-data = `oiExpiry` + `openHighStrategy`; backtest = `HeroZeroPremium` + `OiAttribution`), so the end state matches the prediction with nothing unexplained. Deployed + live-verified 2026-08-01: jars fingerprinted for the new classes, 13/13 containers healthy, engine reloaded 38 published / 0 unresolved / 0 load errors, and each converted handler smoke-tested through the gateway (`OiAnalysis` → `{"items":[]}` with `application/json`, `RiskViews` + `JobViews` typed JSON, option-analytics validation failures returning structured `VALIDATION_FAILED` envelopes rather than SPA HTML). ⚠️ **THE RATCHET ITSELF LEAKED THREE TIMES IN ONE DAY, each time a different blind spot in the same regex** — `ResponseEntity<Map<...>>` (closed #1188), a `java.util.`-qualified return (closed #1191), and the fact that a handler-return regex **cannot see an opaque field inside a typed record at all** (`#SystemStatus.jobs`, `#RiskSettingRow.value` — the latter created by #1191's own conversion). A source-text regex is the wrong instrument for this invariant; the spec-based successor is [#1196](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1196), which measured that at regex-floor-zero edge-gateway still published two unconstrained open objects. Keep the regex as a cheap backstop, but never read its floor as "no opaque responses remain". TWO deliberate non-conversions, assessed not missed: `HeroZeroPremiumController.heroZeroPremium` (POLYMORPHIC — 5 keys empty / 16 populated, so one record would ADD 11 null keys to the empty response: a wire change needing a shape decision) and backtest's `jobs` rollup key (shape owned by the jobs producer). **Every conversion trips ci-contracts' breaking gate by construction** (`additionalProperties:{}` → `$ref` is an incompatible schema replacement to openapi-diff, which cannot tell "became precise" from "key renamed"), so each PR carries `Contract break: APPROVED (…)` — proven per-PR by diffing generated-vs-committed spec: schemas ADDED only, none removed, no other endpoint's responses changed. Other slices untouched; the reference-data constituent SOURCE is still an owner pick. |

Refused trades averaged only **+1.13%** (median −0.68%, win rate 46.0% — near-breakeven, NOT clear winners, unlike the withdrawn rounds' claims). That asymmetry is why only cold starts break and why it hid for months. **Part A — SHIPPED [#874](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/874) @ d9f30a8f, deployed + drill-proven live 07-16.** ⚠️ **BUILT DIFFERENTLY FROM THIS ROW'S ORIGINAL SPEC — do not rebuild to the old text.** The original spec (an OR-clause on the 20s reconcile, `hadResolutionFailures && inSession`) was **rejected**: `reload()` calls `bankCache.clear()` unconditionally (`:317`), so a 20s retry loop through a sustained partial outage would wipe every working strategy's warm ta4j bank every 20s — ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-02 (ledger claim audit shard A): that premise was TRUE when written (2026-07-16) and is FALSE as of today.** [#1226](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1226) @ `e5f465c0` made the eviction SELECTIVE: no `bankCache.clear()` call exists in `SignalEngine` any more (`:941` is only a comment referring to it), and the live behaviour is `SignalEngine.java:995` `bankCache.keySet().removeIf(key -> !freshBankKeys.contains(key))`. The rejection of the original OR-clause spec may still be right on other grounds, but **do not re-derive it from this reason** — the constraint it rests on no longer exists **on the single `signal-eval` thread** — a plausible path into the very eval-starvation stall class we have chased 4× (#634 / A13 RC-1). Built instead: a `kite.status` Redis listener (the channel market-data already publishes; `FeedPipeline.restartFeed:107` self-heals the ticker off the same signal — the engine simply never listened) + a **bounded** 3×~35s retry converging on **`lastReloadEmptyUniverseDrops == 0`** (NOT "loaded > 0": the drop is per-strategy and a breaker re-opening mid-reload leaves a **PARTIAL** load — 32/39 observed live — which a "something loaded" predicate reads as success, stranding the rest silently), plus `start()` reading the level-triggered `kite:session:status` KEY (the channel is edge-only + fire-and-forget, so a `CONNECTED` published before the engine subscribes is lost FOREVER and none ever follows). `futures_screener` is excluded from the drop counter (`resolveScreener` returns empty for BOTH failure and a legitimately empty screen). Bounded by construction ⇒ cannot reintroduce #579. Parity byte-identical (golden 9/9, parity 9/9). **Part B (detection) — BUILT DORMANT via [#1035](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1035) (see G2's `StrategyCoverageWatchdog`, which covers the F10 32-of-39 partial-load class with NOT_LIVE_RESOLVABLE classification; the prerequisite failure-vs-empty split shipped as task_f10a04 #877). Open remainder = the SAME owner arming decision G2 tracks — this row no longer carries independent work.** (Status corrected 2026-07-31 by E2E-01 T4: cell previously said "STILL TODO" and cited #868 `SignalStarvationCanary`, which was RETIRED in #1022.) **NOT a fix:** compose `depends_on: market-data healthy` — container-healthy ≠ term-structure-serving. | 2026-07-16 findings `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-16-session-findings.md` §6.1/§6.3/§7 (drill log in §7.1); `SignalEngine.java` (listener + retry + drop counter), `FuturesTermStructureService.java:78/182`, `SignalStarvationCanary.java:77/87` | clean | **Part A DONE [#874](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/874) · Part B DONE — G2's `StrategyCoverageWatchdog` ARMED live 2026-08-01** |

| G4 | `F-SYNC-instrument-sync-daily-fail` | `INSTRUMENT_SYNC` fails ~08:36 IST daily (`local broker DUMP budget saturated`, 07-13/17/20+): the `kite-dump` limiter is 1 permit/30min, the first attempt burns it then fails on the open circuit, attempts 2–3 have nothing left. **Non-blocking** (registry stays Sunday-fresh from the weekend full sync; options are not sync-gated) but recurring daily noise. Fix = widen the budget, retry after the 30-min refresh, or fix the circuit-open-burns-the-permit interaction | same prose block; promoted 2026-07-25 | clean | **DONE — MERGED [#1023](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1023) 2026-07-26** (3 review rounds, 4 Majors). Root cause was NOT the budget: `KiteCallExecutor.execute` decorated `RateLimiter(CircuitBreaker(call))`, so the permit was acquired BEFORE the breaker was consulted and a breaker-rejected call spent it without touching the network — fatal at 1 permit/30min. Swapped to `CircuitBreaker(RateLimiter(call))` (resilience4j's own documented order). TWO defects that swap introduced or exposed, both caught in review: (a) `RequestNotPermitted` then propagated THROUGH the breaker, so five local saturations could open the SHARED breaker while Kite was healthy — both breakers now `ignore-exceptions` it; (b) preserving the permit did not make the sync RETRY (`CallNotPermittedException` is non-retryable and the 08:30 job runs once), so `morningSyncCatchUp` was added — a bounded 8-attempt sweep 09:05–10:50 IST, skipping only when a sync ended OK *on today's IST date* (`status()` synthesizes an OK from yesterday's `last_seen_at` after a restart, which would otherwise suppress the catch-up entirely). The 1-permit/30min budget is deliberately UNCHANGED. ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-02 — this row's own "registry stays Sunday-fresh from the weekend full sync" premise is FACTUALLY WRONG: no weekly or weekend sync job exists.** `morningSync` (`0 30 8 * * MON-FRI`) and `morningSyncCatchUp` (`0 5,20,35,50 9,10 * * MON-FRI`) are BOTH `MON-FRI`-only (`InstrumentSyncScheduler:31,61`), and `SyncBootstrap` returns early on the `live` profile. `morningSync`'s cron predates #1023 entirely (original commit `051bb780`), so the weekend silence is unrelated to this fix and has existed since the scheduler was written. The two off-schedule SUCCESS rows the premise was inferred from (`2026-07-26 02:37`, `2026-07-27 00:54` IST) each land minutes after a commit and correspond to no cron — incidental manual/deploy-verification triggers, confirmed by grepping every `runSync`/`triggerAsync`/`@Scheduled` call site. **Verdict UNCHANGED (non-blocking):** weekends have no trading and Monday's normal cron + catch-up refresh the registry before the open. **#1023 is confirmed working in production** (`marketdata.ingest_runs`, 12-day window): zero `budget saturated` rows since 07-20, and 07-29 shows `08:29 FAILURE → 09:04 SUCCESS`, i.e. the catch-up recovering a *different*, correctly-non-retried cause (`no live Kite session`). **Residual sub-defect found while verifying, chipped as `task_4fd8eadc` (not shipped):** `KiteCallExecutor.execute` still wraps `Family.DUMP` in its shared internal `Retry` (max 4), so if the one attempt that reaches Kite fails retryably, attempt 2 needs a second permit from a bucket that will not refill for 30 min — it dies on `RequestNotPermitted` and **masks the real cause behind the same "budget saturated" text**. Red-proofed and fixed locally (skip `Retry.decorateSupplier` for `Family.DUMP`), then discarded: zero occurrences in the observed window means it is latent, not active. |
| G5 | `T12-dedicated-quote-limiter` | Futures-OI capture cadence is ~2-min effective (161 single-minute skips): the batched `quotes()` pass queues behind the shared 1/s kite-quote limiter so the serialized cron skips alternate fires. **Owner recommendation = ACCEPT** (consumers tolerate; 3m buckets covered; severity LOW). A dedicated limiter is an owner-visible rate-budget change. **UNPARKED + FIXED 2026-07-26 (owner overrode the ACCEPT recommendation and picked it) — but NOT as a dedicated limiter, because that would have been wrong.** Recon settled three things at source before any code: (1) **Kite documents the quote endpoint at 1 req/second** (kite.trade/docs/connect/v3/exceptions) and `kite-quote` is already configured to exactly that, so a SECOND dedicated 1/s limiter would put us at 2/s — over Kite's published budget. The row's literal framing was unbuildable. (2) The contention source is not ad-hoc page loads: `MonitorSchedulingConfig.barFlushTaskScheduler`'s own javadoc records that one `OptionsSnapshotService` pass is "~70 batched calls ≈ 70 s at the 1/s limit", and it runs every 2 minutes. **CORRECTED at cross-vendor review:** the primary cause is SCHEDULER STARVATION, not the limiter — both passes sat on the single default `taskScheduler` (pool size 1, ~32 methods), so while the options pass held the thread this cron could not even be INVOKED and its fires were dropped before any permit was requested. Limiter fairness only becomes reachable AFTER the isolation. The demand arithmetic was also incomplete: ~35/min (options snapshot) + up to ~24/min (`scheduledBroadcast`, six underlyings × two quote calls) + 1/min here ≈ **60 of 60 permits/min**, not 36 — completion-based `fixedDelay` keeps actual throughput under the ceiling, so capacity is not proven exceeded, but it is NOT comfortably inside either. resilience4j's `AtomicRateLimiter` is not FIFO, so a waiter with the configured 5 s timeout can be starved straight through the options pass's 70-second permit run and abandon the minute. That is the ~2-min effective cadence, exactly. (3) The obvious fix — wait longer — was unsafe on the default pool: `taskScheduler` is **pool size 1 shared by ~32 scheduled methods**, so parking it would trade a capture gap for a service-wide scheduler stall (the S1–S3 failure mode, #1016). **Built instead:** a dedicated single-thread `oiCaptureTaskScheduler` (same fencing rationale as `barFlushTaskScheduler`; NOT `monitorTaskScheduler`, which is reserved for pure detectors) + a bounded 4-attempt / 3 s-backoff patience ladder around the one batched `quotes()` call, retrying ONLY local limiter saturation (a Kite 429, an open breaker or an auth failure still propagates on the first attempt — retrying those spends budget on a call that cannot succeed). **Rate budget is unchanged at 1/s.** New counters `ay_futures_oi_snapshot_quote_retries_total` + `ay_futures_oi_snapshot_skips_total`: retries advancing with skips flat = the fix working; skips advancing = contention outgrew the patience. ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-02 (ledger claim audit shard A):** this line previously wrote the second meter as `..._skips_total`, whose elision names `ay_futures_oi_snapshot_quote_skips_total` — **a meter that does not exist.** The real name has no `quote_` segment (`FuturesOiSnapshotService.java:70-71`, confirmed live on `/actuator/prometheus`). An operator following the old text greps for nothing and reads the absent meter as FLAT, i.e. as the fix working — failure in the safe-looking direction. `MonitorSchedulingConfig.java:122` already spelled it correctly; only this row was wrong. | `2026-07-25-weekly-bug-queue.md` B9 + item 5 | clean (no budget change) | **DONE — MERGED [#1031](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1031) 2026-07-26, deployed live.** 8/8 `FuturesOiSnapshotServiceTest` green (3 new: retry-then-succeed, bounded-skip-counted, non-saturation-not-retried). ✅ **LIVE-VERIFIED on the first full forward session, 2026-07-27: cadence 372 of 375 minutes (99.2%)**, against 211 / 198 / 187 / 192 / 208 over the five preceding sessions — the ~2-min effective cadence is gone. OI quadrants live a 5th consecutive session (`2026-07-27-session-findings.md` §4) |

The concurrency cliff sits EXACTLY at ₹15,000. **No republish and no migration: the scalper YAMLs came out byte-identical to main.** **[#1075](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1075) is now ONLY the ₹20,000 raise (HOLD, OWNER) — cross-vendor APPROVED at `69a949af` after SIX rounds:** the `budget_inr` ₹15,000→₹20,000 raise + the deployment cap and per-sub-account ceiling, all three landed at ONE seam (`PaperService.openOrder`, the sole writer — emission cannot gate them because the rail needs the candidate's SIZE, which does not exist yet). **The review found 4 further defects after the first two, THREE of them created by my own fixes:** refusing straddle leg 2 left leg 1 open = a NAKED directional position (fixed by an atomic `openPair`); both caps were unlocked read-check-write races (per-book advisory lock); sub-account SELECTION still ran outside that lock so 5 accounts behaved as 1 (assignment moved under the lock); and the new wrapper took `book → anchor` while the manual path takes `anchor → book` — a **DEADLOCK on the money path** (fixed by `lockAnchorsBeforeBook`; round 6 confirmed by global scan that no `book → anchor` path remains). Same shape as #1067's "a dead path hides every defect downstream of it". **OWNER DECISION: ₹20,000 against a ₹30,000 sub-account allocation means each account holds ONE position, so scalper concurrency becomes 5** (book cap would allow 6, `max_open` says 20); at ₹15,000 the binding limit was 8. The raise REDUCES concurrency 8→5 — bigger positions, fewer of them. Impact is now LOW (the 09:15 bar clears every floor by 2.4×), so this is a code read, not an incident | `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-27-session-findings.md` §6.1 / T23 | clean | ✅✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-01 — pair-aware suppression MERGED [#1180](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1180) @ 6a312403, deployed + live-verified (thread dump shows `partial-bucket-sched-1` distinct from `monitor-sched-1`).** The straddle is now recognised as ONE benign unit: a non-benign bucket is held one bucket and the PAIR is suppressed together (one INFO + a straddle counter), never one half alone. Lot size resolves from the instrument master, never a hardcoded 65, and fails CLOSED when unresolvable. **Prerequisite finding that shaped the design: re-deriving a lost hold is IMPOSSIBLE at any look-back depth** — `CandleQueryService.java:103-105` shows 1m reads the base table and 3m is a rollup over that same table, so post-restart both sides compare equal by construction and the diverging side (live tick-agg, memory-only) died with the process. The fix therefore carries the OBSERVATION in a durable Redis HASH, not a position. ⚠️ **FIVE review rounds found SIX ways a live pager could go SILENT, every one introduced by an individually-correct fix and none reachable by a green suite:** asymmetric 25% cap (capped the held half, not the incoming partner, so a genuinely frozen bucket corroborated as benign); restart losing a held mismatch; a one-bucket look-back that still skipped it when two buckets completed; a TTL key under `volatile-lru` (evictable, plus read-failure indistinguishable from absence); **delete-before-report** (a crash after Redis accepted the delete but before the log erased the alarm — now report-first, so a crash duplicates rather than erases); and **recovery indexed by live series**, so a rolled-away contract's alarm was unreachable — the case persistence exists for (restart) combined with the thing that changes across one (which contracts are live). A proactive audit then found a THIRD lose-information site: `classified.put()` marked a bucket handled BEFORE acting on it. **`volume-tolerance-pct` STAYS 0.0** even now: the pct arm is per-bucket, so at 5% it absorbs the thick half while firing the thin one, manufacturing a false UNPAIRED WARN (23,000 vs 7,062, pinned by test). Eleven defect shapes enumerated and red-proofed; operators should expect MORE WARNs after a restart, not fewer (fail-closed on a cold lot cache). **Method lesson: TWO of this PR's three red-proofs were WRONG when first written** — fixtures that satisfied their assertion through an unrelated path, invisible to a green run, caught only because a deliberate mutation failed to red. History: RE-NARROWED TWICE; the residue is provably benign and the ROW HAD BECOME A TOLERANCE ROW, not a correctness one. 07-28: WARNs tripled to 10, the 09:15 signature did NOT recur, and the 2 unpaired events moved to the session's two thickest buckets (+2,730 / −2,600 = 42 and 40 lots). **2026-07-29 is the cleanest session in the series: 6 WARNs, ALL of them exact ± pairs on consecutive buckets, ALL shortfalls exact ×65 multiples, ZERO unpaired events** — the documented benign boundary straddle (README §3.17), with no unexplained residue left at either end. **The 09:15 signature #981 targeted has now failed to recur on two consecutive warm-process sessions.** What survives is a tolerance defect: the largest shortfall (**16,835 = 259 lots**) is **3.5%** of a 476,840 opening bar — comparable to 07-28's 2.5%/2.4% and 07-27's 2.7% — so every one of the 6 events was provably benign **and every one still alarmed**, because `artha.signals.partial-bucket-canary.volume-tolerance` is an ABSOLUTE 650 (+ a ≤10% relative arm). **Proposal, unchanged in substance since 07-28 and now strongly evidenced: make the tolerance scale with bar size.** ⚠ Do NOT simply raise the absolute — a thin frozen bar must still fire, which is why the relative arm exists; the change is to the ABSOLUTE arm's basis, not its removal. The post-#981 rollover-baseline code read is still outstanding but is now LOW value: two clean negative controls make a second contributor at the open unlikely. `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-29-session-findings.md` §6.1 / T23. **DELIVERED (PR [#1180](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1180)):** the fix is NOT a bigger number — the per-event gate was reading each bucket in isolation while §3.17's benign signature is a PAIR property, so a non-benign bucket is now HELD one bucket and suppressed TOGETHER with its equal-and-opposite partner (both halves or neither — a half-suppressed pair presents as the unpaired shape the runbook teaches operators to read as a real defect). Corroboration needs all five of: immediately-following bucket, opposite signs, **BOTH halves ≤25% of their OWN bucket** (so a frozen bar is never explained away, whichever side it lands on — the cross-vendor review found the cap was applied only to the held half, which let a genuine frozen partner be cancelled by an earlier small skew), both magnitudes exact multiples of the contract lot (instrument master, never a hardcoded 65; a lot not yet resolved keeps the rule CLOSED), and an uncancelled residue inside BOTH `volume-tolerance` and 10% of the larger half — so the suppression can hide at most the absolute tolerance the canary already treats as noise, adding no new blind spot. **A hold is taken only if it was stored durably first** (Redis, no migration; `save` refusing ⇒ WARN now, so Redis-down degrades to exactly pre-G9 behaviour), and the carried observation is restored on the next cold sweep. ⚠️ **Stored with NO TTL, deliberately** — the instance runs `maxmemory-policy volatile-lru` (`deploy/docker-compose.yml:72-83`) under which only keys carrying an expiry are eviction candidates, so a TTL'd unreported observation could be dropped by memory pressure *or* the clock; the key is deleted on RESOLUTION instead. A read FAILURE is tri-stated as UNKNOWN (never "absent"), so the restore is retried and the canary refuses to defer until it knows — one Redis blip can no longer swallow a mismatch. **Write-ahead ordering throughout**: the counter/log fires BEFORE the durable delete, so a crash between them can duplicate a report but never erase one (three sites had the losing order — both resolution paths and, found by auditing for a third instance, the `classified` marker, which retired a bucket before it had been acted on). Recovery enumerates the durable index (one Redis HASH, `HGETALL`) **independently of the live series set** and reports any hold whose contract is gone — indexing by live series meant a monthly roll stranded an unreported mismatch for ever, since "is this alarm reachable" and "is this contract still trading" are unrelated questions. The canary also has its **own scheduler** (`partialBucketTaskScheduler`): it left the `monitorTaskScheduler` detector fence when it acquired external dependencies, and every store call is time-bounded (2 s) on a bounded-queue executor with cancel-on-timeout, so a stalled Redis cannot starve sibling detectors, pile up abandoned writes, or resurrect a resolved observation. ⚠️ **Re-deriving a lost hold is impossible, so no look-back of any depth would have worked** — the 3m series is `rangeRolledFromOneMinute` over the very same `candles` 1m rows the 1m series is re-warmed from (`CandleQueryService:103-105`), so after a restart a re-read compares DB against DB and is benign by construction; the diverging side (live tick-agg, never revised) died with the process. Also: the lot lookup is a NON-BLOCKING cache with an off-pool prefetch — the sweep runs on the single-threaded monitor scheduler whose contract forbids blocking external calls. **Honest latency:** a >25% skew still WARNs by T+60 s unchanged; a same-sign drift by T+4 m; a never-arriving partner by T+5 m. All six 07-29 events replay to 0 WARNs / 3 straddles (red-proofed at 6.0 against the pre-change logic); unpaired, same-sign-drift, mismatched-magnitude, non-lot-multiple, never-arriving-partner, **capped-partner frozen bucket**, **held half across a multi-bucket restart**, **undurable hold**, **unreadable store**, **crash between report and durable delete** and **hold on a rolled-away contract** shapes each still WARN, every one mutation- or red-proofed. ⚠️ A cold process's lot cache is EMPTY, so the first pair after a restart WARNs both halves rather than suppressing — two safe behaviours composing into extra noise, never silence; pinned so the enumeration cannot drift into claiming a suppression guarantee that only holds with a pre-warmed fixture. New meter `ay_signal_partial_bucket_straddle_total`. **Complementary to, not a substitute for, [#1168](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1168)** — that PR rebases the absolute arm on bar size, this one changes what counts as ONE event; `volume-tolerance-pct` is untouched here |
| G10 | `T27-relative-floor-opening-surge` | **The armed relative volume floor's SHAPE is mis-calibrated for the first ~90 minutes of every session, and the opening surge is the mechanism.** `ScalperGates.relativeVolumeFloor` (`ScalperGates.java:188-202`) resolves the floor as `multiplier × MEDIAN(priorVolumes)` — robust to ONE outlier, but the open is not one bar. Measured 2026-07-29 on `NIFTY26AUGFUT` 3m: **4 of the first ten buckets were ≥100,000** (476,840 / 153,075 / 124,410 / 105,560) against a **session median of 15,015**, so the median itself is legitimately high and stays high until the surge bars roll out of the window. Thresholds ran **133,185 at 09:45–09:57 — the p98.4 of the operand's own session distribution** (only 2 of 125 bars exceed it) — decaying monotonically to ~26,000 by 11:21, while the operand sat at 11,000–34,000 throughout. **Consequence: 326 of the session's 756 `volume-floor` blocks (43%) landed before 11:00**, a ~1h15m slice of a ~5h45m session — and the slice in which most of the fleet first comes in-window (most scalper YAMLs open after 09:45). **STRUCTURAL by construction: the opening surge recurs every session.** Candidate fixes (pick after the code read, do not guess): exclude the opening ~30 min from the window · seed the window from the PRIOR session's median · normalise the baseline by time-of-day. ⚠️ **FIRST STEP IS A CODE READ OF `priorVolumes`, NOT A NUMBER.** The exact window length/offset was **NOT reconstructible from SQL** — two observed thresholds reconcile exactly to `1.5 × median` of a 10-bar DB window (87,799 = median{09:15…09:42}; 72,004 = median{09:24…09:51}) but a third (46,118 at 10:27) matches no window tried, and the engine reads its own in-memory `LiveSeriesStore` series which `PartialBucketCanary` proves diverges from the DB rollup. The finding rests on the OBSERVED thresholds and the OBSERVED operand distribution, both measured directly; the window reconstruction is explicitly not part of it. ⚠️ **DO NOT CONFLATE WITH T1 (G1).** T1 is the MULTIPLIER and is REJECTED on the same session's counterfactual (2W/9L); this row is the WINDOW. The floor's level is vindicated and its shape is not — two different fixes, and applying the T1 knob would make this row worse, not better | `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-29-session-findings.md` §2.1 / T27; code read `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-29-g10-relative-volume-window.md` | **HOLD (changes which signals fire — same tier as G6)** | ✅✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-01 — owner accepted the DO-NOT-ARM verdict (weekend decision pack); the flag stays default-OFF, re-openable on new evidence.** Prior state: **MEASURED, BUILT (default-OFF) and P&L'd 2026-07-29/30 — VERDICT: DO NOT ARM.** [#1119](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1119) @ `f6ba4dab`, deployed + live-verified unarmed (0 rows in `strategy.strategies` carry the tag). Full detail below; the short form is: the opening bias is REAL (21 sessions, mean 3.52×, over-estimating on 19 of 21) but the trades behind it LOSE MONEY (265 legs, +324.87 gross → **−590.95 after 1% cost**, sign flips on 5 legs). Original code-read step DONE 2026-07-29. ⚠️ **The mechanism is a LEVEL SHIFT, not an outlier problem — a different diagnosis from the one filed, and it eliminates one of the three candidate fixes.** The row blamed "4 of the first ten buckets ≥100,000"; those four are not the cause. On 07-29 the **minimum** of the first ten 3m bars was **32,760 — already 2.2× the session median of 15,015**. Every bar in the opening window is elevated, so its median is elevated; a median is the right tool against outliers and the wrong one against a level shift, and no robustness rescues it. **Parameters now pinned and live-confirmed:** window **20** bars, minBars **10**, multiplier **1.5** (`ScalperOiProps:102-104`), `priorVolumes` = bars `index-1…index-window` from the in-memory bank, **truncating at the series start** (`ScalperConfluenceGate:1213-1219`). No YAML, `application.yml` or container-env override exists — verified all three, so the defaults ARE live. **The row's unreconciled threshold now reconciles exactly:** at deploy bar 09:45 the 20-bar window truncates to the 10 bars available, median = (53,040+64,025)/2 = **58,532.5**, ×1.5 = **87,798.75** = the observed **87,799** (independently confirmed by `percentile_cont(0.5)` over those bars). The other two thresholds still do not reconcile against DB-rolled bars — expected and already documented (the engine reads `LiveSeriesStore`, which `PartialBucketCanary` proves diverges); not pursued, the finding never rested on them. **Also newly quantified: the pre-09:45 regime is WORSE and is not the relative floor at all** — below minBars the floor is the **absolute 125,000** NIFTY fallback, which only **2 of 125** bars clear all session, so the floor *drops* at 09:45 (125,000 → 87,799) before decaying; any account of the 43%-before-11:00 figure must attribute part of it to the fallback. Decay is mechanical and datable: bar 0 leaves the 20-bar window at **10:18**, the last ≥100k bar at **10:42**. **Candidate fixes, two now decided:** "exclude the opening 30 min" is **REJECTED on its own** (it drops the window below minBars and falls through to the stricter 125,000 fallback — it makes the row worse); ~~"seed the window from the PRIOR session's median" is the recommendation~~ **WITHDRAWN — measured 2026-07-29 and it REVERSES the bias rather than fixing it** (see `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-29-g10-baseline-bakeoff.md`): a FLAT baseline cannot track a shape that rises at the open, so the seed swings the open from 2.6× HARDER than the rest of the day to **2× EASIER** (57.8% open pass vs 29.5% rest) — it would admit exactly the low-quality opening entries the floor exists to filter. **The TIME-OF-DAY PROFILE wins** (28.8% open vs 36.3% rest, ratio 0.79 — closest to uniform of the three; status quo 0.39, seed 1.96). Status-quo bias now confirmed at 21× the original sample: opening-10 median over session median is **mean 3.52×, worst 8.42×, over-estimating on 19 of 21 sessions and never once under** — systematic, not a bad-day artifact. ⚠️ A longer lookback does NOT rescue a flat baseline (rolling-5 is no better on the worst case and WORSE on days over 1.5×) — the SHAPE is the thing, not the level. ⚠️ Roll confound checked and cleared: the front-future roll is gradual over ~3 sessions, not a cliff. ✅ **BUILT default-OFF ([#1119](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1119)) and P&L'd — VERDICT: DO NOT ARM.** The profile does exactly what it was built to do and the entries it unlocks LOSE MONEY. 265 distinct legs over 10 sessions (vs G13's 6 — `volume-floor` binds on 88% of blocks so the sample is decisive), 265/265 priced: **117W/148L, +324.87 pts gross**, but **ex-top-5-legs −305.88** (the sign flips on 1.9% of legs), **median leg −1.65**, and **−590.95 after a 1% round-trip cost**. Gross edge +1.23/leg vs ~3.5/leg cost on a 345 premium; break-even ≈ **0.35%** (computed). ⚠️ **This reframes the row's own finding**: the opening window IS systematically restrictive (21 sessions, mean 3.52x, 19 of 21) — but the trades behind that restriction are unprofitable, so the bias was effectively PROTECTING the book. ⚠️⚠️ **FOURTH independent test to reject a loosening of this entry gate**: T1 (multiplier, 2W/9L), T7 (threshold, worst book −₹321/close), G13 (IV bloc, undecidable at 6 legs), G10 (profile, negative after costs). **Every measured loosening on this track has lost money — that is the prior for any future proposal here.** ⚠️ All four were measured under the 30-minute `time_stop` that **G11** says is itself the dominant P&L term — if G11 changes the exit, re-run every one of them. ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-02 (task_2735acfb): "all four were measured under the 30-minute `time_stop`" is wrong twice.** (a) **False for T7** in the opposite direction — T7's only forward evidence is the `composite-055` SHADOW book, which by construction replicates brackets + structural stop + 15:12 square-off but **NOT** the YAML `time_stop` (§3.16; `2026-07-29-session-findings.md:503-505`); T7 was measured under NO time stop and is the one of the four this correction leaves horizon-independent. (b) For the other three the "30-minute `time_stop`" is a **harness parameter** (`ExitKnobs.timeStopBars`, wall-MINUTES on the option's 1m premium, uniform because a COUNTERFACTUAL job carries no strategy version), not the fleet's armed rule (3m bars on the index future, per strategy, 6th of 7 in precedence) — see G11. **G10's own verdict is unaffected:** its two decisive legs of evidence are horizon-independent, and an independent sweep at 30/36/48/60/90 min reproduces the concentration finding at EVERY horizon (`net ex-top-5` never turns positive across all sessions). Subtracting a trailing median mechanically forces a zero-median, sign-symmetric, oscillating series regardless of input. Root-threshold split narrows to 1.26x (vs R1's 1.8x), so a single fleet-wide knob WOULD be defensible for a residual — recorded, but not what kills it. **Do not re-propose either operand.** Successor question (unmeasured, needs forward paper on real captured OI, not more slicing of these 15–22 sessions): whether the p90 band is genuinely dead or merely not-shown-open. Evidence: `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-02-tune-verdicts-vs-time-stop-spread.md`. ⚠️ **My shadow-variant proposal was WRONG and is withdrawn** — `ShadowVariants` is per-RAIL overrides + a composite FLOOR re-scored against an already-computed composite (`ShadowVariants.java:19-27`); dropping a DOT changes the composite COMPUTATION upstream in `ConnectTheDotsScorer`, which a variant cannot express. Per-session maxima 6/5/6/7/7/6/6 across 07-20…29 — **every session reaches ≥5.** The cause is slug fan-out, not coincidence: every scalper shares one 3m NIFTY-future series and one `StrikePicker`, so a signalling bar resolves a whole ~6-wide family onto the SAME ATM leg. **There is no mechanism that produces exactly 3**, so the row's 3-passes/4-refused boundary sits in the empty part of the histogram; the real cases are 5 = ₹48,205, 6 = ₹57,846, 7 = ₹67,487 against the ₹30,000 sub-account cap (1.6–2.2×). **15 of 77 measured (bar,key) events would hit the ceiling.** ⚠️ **NOT binding today, and the reason is the FIRE RATE, not the bound** — the paper book opens on FIRES and fired signals reach at most **2**-way convergence (07-20/27/29: max 1/2/2, zero keys ≥3), which is why #1086 refused nothing on day one. It is `@Scheduled`-only, and `SwingBatchCatchUp.java:43` explicitly REJECTED boot catch-up. That rejection was reasoned from an EVENING boot (2026-07-17 booted 22:14, screens landed 22:15 — inputs did not exist yet) and **does not transfer to a MORNING boot inside 08:35–09:15**, when the prior session's bar is final and its screen has been persisted since ~19:31. `computed` 2026-08-14: the machine booted **08:38**, three minutes past the cron; `swing_catchup_runs` had **no row at all** for `session_date=2026-08-13` (the sweep never even seeded), `risk_audit` had **0 rows**, and the entries would have been lost permanently — Monday's sweep withholds entries once the funnel is no longer that session's screen, then burns attempt budget to ABANDONED. **Recovered manually** the same morning (owner-approved): logs snapshotted, `ARTHA_SWING_CATCHUP_CRON` overridden to `0 58 8` via shell env (compose substitution outranks `.env`, so the hardened-ACL `.env` was never touched), `up -d --force-recreate --no-deps strategy-signal-service` from the REAL checkout. The pinned 08-13 sweep then ran at 08:58 and took **4 entries / 3 positions** (manas CUPID 65@295.01, manas RPEL 13@1389.49, minervini CUPID 31@295.01) with 26 minutes to spare before `marketOpenDeadlinePassed()`. ⚠️ **The SAME defect shape was found the same hour by the cross-vendor review of [#1373](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1373)** — its 16:05 population prefetch is also cron-only, so a late boot means the pass never runs for that session and `compared` collapses (202 → 14). ⚠️ **The reviewer's stated MECHANISM for that finding is FALSE and was measured false the same hour — record the correction, not just the fix.** Codex argued a late boot lets `BhavcopyStartupCatchup` claim the buckets first, and that `upsertAuthoritativeAll`'s keep-existing-source rule then drops the perfectly-agreeing bars out of the `source='KITE'` population. `computed` on 2026-08-13's NSE 1d rows: **KITE rows carry `oi = 0` (23 of 23, zero NULLs) and BHAVCOPY rows carry `oi = NULL` (2689 of 2689, zero zeros)**. The keep-source branch requires o/h/l/c/volume AND `oi` to all match, and `NULL IS NOT DISTINCT FROM 0` is FALSE — so a Kite write landing after a bhavcopy write ALWAYS flips `source` to KITE. Bhavcopy-first is therefore safe too, and the ordering bias is ~zero rather than the PR body's ~3.2% upper bound (22/682 was close-equality alone, which is not the same predicate). **The 16:05-not-18:50 ordering argument in #1373's body is overstated for the same reason.** The defect is real and the fix is right; only the reason was wrong. ⚠️ Note the safety is ACCIDENTAL — it rests on the two sources encoding "no open interest" differently. If a bhavcopy projection ever wrote `oi = 0`, or a Kite equity bar ever wrote NULL, the ordering hazard becomes real with no test guarding it. **Two instances of one doctrine gap: a cron-only job on a machine that is off 19:00–08:00 needs a replay path, or it fails silently in the safe-looking direction.** Owner decision 2026-08-14: reuse the existing owner-armed `artha.swing.catchup-enabled`, add an `ApplicationReadyEvent` listener that runs the SAME sweep only when boot lands after the configured cron time and before `SESSION_OPEN`, reusing the existing atomic claim + market-open deadline so it cannot double-emit | measured live 2026-08-14; `SwingBatchCatchUp.java:43,174,283`; #1373 review round 1 | clean (money path — it fires real entries) | **OPEN — fix approved, build in flight.** ⚠️ Until it ships, a boot after 08:35 forfeits that day's entries with NO alert; the tell is `swing_catchup_runs` having no row for the prior session. |
