**2026-08-13 · THE SWING BOOKS ARE LOCKED, AND IT IS NOT AN EXIT DEFECT.** Owner-triggered
measurement; full findings in `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-13-swing-exit-stickiness.md`. 18 open
positions, 20 would-enter candidates on the 08:35 catch-up, **0 admitted**. Verdict: **HOLD is
correct on all 18** — zero NULL stops, `exit_skipped = 0`, 16 of 18 profitable, **+₹27,214** marked,
median ~12% above the effective exit; only minervini INOXINDIA is close, at 0.78%. Neither book
declares a `take_profit` or `time_stop` in its PUBLISHED config, so nothing should have fired at 37
days. Two framing corrections to my own question: the 6% open-risk cap is **manas-only** (minervini
is blocked by a 12-slot cap, at 12/12), and `strategy.sell_decisions` is a **survivor log** written
after the passes — all 498 rows ever written are HOLD, so a 100%-HOLD table carries no signal at all.

Three structural findings, all on the RISK side:
- **manas sits at 99.91% of cap — ₹7.55 of headroom** (₹8,569.23 vs ₹8,576.78), and it is arithmetic:
  sizing is 1%/trade against a 6% cap, so **1% × 6 = 6% exactly and the 7th declared slot is
  unreachable by construction.** ⚠️ **CORRECTION, same day, by the builder sent to fix it:** I wrote
  here that growing equity cannot help because "both terms are percentages of equity, so the ratio is
  scale-invariant". **That is wrong.** Already-open risk is ABSOLUTE — banked in rupees at entry —
  so only the FUTURE lot scales with equity. Restoring the missing ₹27,214 moves headroom
  ₹7.55 → **₹553**, which is real but still short of a 1% lot. The conclusion (the 7th slot does not
  open) survives; the reasoning that produced it did not. **A ratio argument is only scale-invariant
  if BOTH operands actually scale — check that the one you are dividing by is not already banked.**
- **`openRiskInr` measures `avgEntry − stop`**, so appreciation never releases budget. The one
  release valve (`ManasGoverningStopCache`) was *provably* inert on 2026-08-13: entries run before
  exits (`SwingBatchEngine.java:316` vs `:321`) and the container booted 08:03 before the 08:35 run.
  The arithmetic confirms rather than assumes it — a warm cache would have dropped risk to 3.05% and
  **admitted** the entry that was refused.
- **The equity denominator cannot see the ₹27,214 of unrealized gain.** `unrealizedTotal` marks via
  Redis `ticks:last`, verified independently: `HLEN` = 307, every entry an NFO/BFO option contract,
  and `HEXISTS ticks:last NSE:<sym>` = 0 for every swing equity. The term silently evaluates to
  **₹0.00**, so the cap binds tighter than designed. Another N44/#14.

**Owner decision 2026-08-13: fix the blind denominator AND the unreachable 7th slot; leave
`avgEntry − stop` alone for now** (that one is doctrine, not a bug). HOLD tier — built as #1368, PR
left OPEN.

⚠️ **AND THEN THE BUILD MEASURED THE FIX AND FOUND IT CHANGES NOTHING TODAY: 0 of 20 admitted
before, 0 of 20 after — neither fix, nor both together.** Two independent reasons, both of which the
investigation above missed. **(1) 16 of the 20 candidates are minervini, which has NO RISK CAP AT
ALL** (`PyramidPolicy.NONE`) — it is locked purely by its 12/12 slot cap, so no equity or sizing
change can touch it. The finding above correctly said the 6% cap is manas-only, then failed to carry
that through to "so fixing the cap cannot help the 16". **(2) Shrinking the NEXT lot to 0.8% does not
refund the ₹8,569 already banked by six positions sized at 1.0%** — the 7th manas slot opens after
**four position turnovers**, not on deploy. **The lesson is the one this ledger keeps relearning:
measure the FIX against the real population before shipping it, not just the DEFECT.** Both defects
were real; the remedy was inert.

**A fourth lever LOOKED like it would work immediately and changes no declared parameter: warming
`ManasGoverningStopCache`.** Open risk drops to ₹4,356 — 2.87% against the 6% cap (⚠️ the
doc-of-record says **3.05%** for the same ₹4,356; one of the two is wrong and it is unresolved).
Inert today only because entries run before exits (`SwingBatchEngine.java:316` vs `:321`) and the
cache is cold on every boot.

⚠️⚠️ **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-13, TWICE OVER — DO NOT BUILD IT. This paragraph recommended it for
several hours after it had been withdrawn, which is exactly the drift this ledger exists to stop.**

**(a) It is infeasible as specified.** Built once, cross-vendor review found FOUR Criticals, all in
the exit path; reverted. A feasibility pass then produced two independent impossibility results.
*Criterion 3 is the negation of the warm's own precondition*: the warm needs the daily series BEFORE
`entryPass`, the exit pass fetches it AFTER, and exactly two reconciliations exist — a separate fetch
(built; ≤180 s exit delay, deadline consumption, two samples that can disagree) or sharing
`seriesCache` (one sample, but the exit then consumes a series sampled at warm time). **There is no
third option.** The withdrawn attempt avoided the shared cache *deliberately to protect the exit*,
and that separate fetch is what broke it. *Criterion 4 guarantees the equity mark fails open*: an
unmarked position falls back to `avgEntryPrice`, erasing its unrealized **in whichever direction it
sits**, so an unmarked LOSER overstates equity and loosens every `pct`-mode rail. Fail-soft ⇒ partial
marks ⇒ unbounded-sign error; correct semantics is all-or-nothing, which is not fail-soft.
**Corollary worth keeping: the two caches must NOT be warmed by one mechanism — stop-cache
partiality is monotonically conservative, equity-mark partiality is not.**

**(b) It would have been WRONG even if feasible, and this is the part that matters.** The published
manas trail carries `breakeven_floor: true` with `arm_pct: 9` (**verified**: both
`manas-arora-breakout` and `manas-arora-vcp`). An ARMED trail therefore sits at or above entry, so
the warm would collapse counted open risk almost to fill slippage — **the warm does not adjust the
cap, it very nearly switches it off.** Measured 2026-08-13 on the 4 open manas positions (persisted
stops total **₹5,708.50**): peak gains **8.08 / 11.97 / 13.84 / 18.01%**, so **3 of 4 armed** and
roughly **74% of counted risk released**. ⚠️ The feasibility doc claims 4 of 4 and ~99.4%
(₹5,708.50 → ≤₹32.19) from a 10.25% low-end; KANORICHEM measures **8.08%** here, below the arm
threshold. Direction agrees, magnitude does not — **unresolved, and worth settling before anyone
acts on either figure.** Above all: **the warm swaps an ENFORCEABLE stop (`stop_loss`, polled every
15 s by the bracket sweep) for an UNENFORCEABLE one (the trail, run once daily and explicitly
exit-neutral) at the one moment it decides money.**

**The cheapest correct fix is a SIDE TABLE written at the existing `cacheGoverningStop` call site** —
already inside the no-exit branch, already downstream of the mixed-lot refusal, no fetch, no ordering
change, already inside the fail-soft try. It satisfies all four criteria trivially. ⚠️ **The settled
rejection may not actually cover it:** that ruling was against writing to `stop_loss` and against a
new column on `paper_positions`, and a side table is neither. Owner's call. If it stays closed, the
fallback is the **degraded-mode signal** — the rail's own report says it is running blind rather
than quietly under-reporting.

⚠️ **`ARTHA_PAPER_RISK_PER_TRADE_RISK_PCT` DOES NOT DRIVE SIZING.** It feeds exactly one site — the
advisory `advised_lots` DISPLAY column. Real sizing comes from the published config's
`risk_pct_equity`. Both currently read `1.0`, which is precisely why the error was invisible:
**flipping that env var to retune sizing would have changed nothing but a display field**, and the
books would have looked mysteriously unmoved. The 2026-08-13 exit-stickiness doc cites it wrongly;
this is the correction.

**N71 · A COVERAGE BOARD THAT BUCKETS BY WHEN A JOB RAN CANNOT SEE WORK DONE BY CATCH-UP — AND IT
FAILS IN THE ALARMING DIRECTION.** 2026-08-13, owner-reported: `/api/v1/market/health/ingest` showed
MINERVINI_SCREEN and MANAS_SCREEN **RED** for exactly one day, 2026-08-12, detail *"no ingest run
recorded for the trading day"*. **No data was missing.** `screen_date = 2026-08-12` holds 2270 manas
and 1785 minervini rows, matching the `rows_written` of runs that finished 08:04 IST the next
morning. `IngestCoverageCanary.java:606` selects by `started_at`, so the catch-up's work stamped
08-13, fell outside the 07-30→08-12 window, and left 08-12 looking empty. **The board asks "did a run
START on this calendar day" when the meaningful question is "does output EXIST for this trade
date"** — and those diverge precisely when catch-up saves you, i.e. every day under a 19:00 shutdown
with a 19:30 chain head. 08-10 and 08-11 read GREEN only by luck: deploy-triggered boot catch-ups
happened to land at 18:48 and 18:01, same calendar day. The precedent for the fix already exists in
the same file — `BHAVCOPY_BOTH_EXCHANGES_ADVANCED` queries `nse_eod_bhavcopy` by `trade_date`
(`:629`), and `CAPTURE` keys on `window_start` rather than `started_at`. Same family as the
`series='EQ'` filter artifact: **it is the rare trap that fails LOUD, which makes it burn
investigation time rather than hide a defect.**

**N70 · THE SCREENS DO NOT RUN ON THEIR CRON, AND I ASSERTED A CRON TIME TWICE BEFORE CHECKING.**
2026-08-13. `ManasScheduler` has THREE entry points — `@EventListener(ApplicationReadyEvent)` at
`:54`, `@EventListener(BhavcopyBackfillCompleted.class)` at `:60`, and `@Scheduled` at `:66` — and
the one that fires is the **event**. Every observed run is bhavcopy-chained, with Minervini starting
the same second Manas finishes (08-07 19:31:11→19:31:41→19:32:21; 08-10 18:48:38→…; 08-11 18:00:47→…;
08-13 08:04:06→…). The 19:50/19:55 crons have not fired since the shutdown policy began, because
`ARTHA_BHAVCOPY_EOD_CRON=0 30 19` is itself past 19:00. **Consequence for #1358: the lever is the
BHAVCOPY move to 18:45; the 18:47/18:48 screen cron moves are a redundant safety net, not the
mechanism.** My own errors, both corrected: I first told the owner "19:31 cron" (that was an OBSERVED
run time, not a schedule), then read the env `0 50 19` as authoritative without asking why observed
runs disagreed with it. **A schedule and a fire time are different measurements; when they disagree,
something else is driving the job — enumerate every trigger on the class before naming a cause.**

**N69 · A GUARD AGAINST CROSS-TEST BLEED BOUNDED ON ONE SIDE IS HALF A GUARD.** 2026-08-13, CI red on
#1361's market-data shard in a test that PR does not touch: `IndexConstituentsIntegrationTest`
expected `[2026-06-10, 2026-06-11]` and got `[2026-03-02, 2026-06-10, 2026-06-11]`. The assertion
**already knew** about shared-singleton-DB bleed and filtered for it — `!d.isAfter(2026-06-11)` — and
its comment names the reasoning: *"another IT's context boot can accrue a TODAY-dated snapshot"*. The
reasoning was right and stopped at one edge. A sibling with a PAST-dated `@Primary` Clock
(`ContinuousFuturesBackfillIntegrationTest` pins 2026-03-02) walks straight underneath.
**Contamination has no preferred direction: scope to a WINDOW, never a ceiling.** Fixed in #1364 by
bounding both sides AND seeding the contaminant through the real accrual path, so the isolation is
deterministic rather than dependent on surefire ordering — the red-proof then reproduces CI's message
verbatim. Third instance of this family after #1357 and #405.

**N68 · A GATE'S OPERAND CAN BE DESTROYED BY A CHANGE IN A DIFFERENT JOB, AND SEVERITY KEYED ON THE
NUMERATOR WILL NEVER NOTICE.** 2026-08-13. `BhavcopyCloseCanary` compares NSE bhavcopy closes against
Kite 1d candles. Its denominator collapsed **165 → 15** on 2026-08-11 (`computed`: 08-05=176,
08-06=164, 08-07=165, 08-10=165, 08-11=15, 08-12=15) — landing exactly on #1333, which moved the
swing batch from a ~20:00 full-universe evening run to a 16:00 exits-only settle. **The canary never
had its own bar population**; it was reading a side effect of whatever the swing batch happened to
fetch, a dependency `MinerviniSwingScheduler`'s javadoc documents outright. Against 3308 NSE EQ
bhavcopy rows it now checks ~0.4% of the universe and reports **GREEN**, because status is driven
only by `divergent` vs `redFloor` and never by `compared` being small. Pure N44/#14. ⚠️ **And note
which direction I got wrong:** I had raised this as a false-ALARM risk on #1358. It is the opposite —
a success-shaped nothing. **When you predict a gate will misfire, check which way: an operand that
shrinks makes a numerator-keyed gate quieter, not louder.** Floor being built; the real fix is giving
the comparison its own population.

**N67 · A CRON MINUTE EXPRESSES ONLY WHEN A JOB STARTS — NOT WHAT WINDOW IT READS, WHAT IT WAITS
FOR, OR WHICH THREAD IT BLOCKS.** 2026-08-12, #1358: moving three jobs across the 19:00 boundary
produced three Criticals, and all three were the same mistake in different clothes. (1) The paper
reconciler's window was `today 00:00 -> now`; correct at 21:15, and at 08:50 it covers a morning on
which nothing has traded — every bounded query returns zero rows, every check passes, and the run row
records **a clean reconciliation of an empty set**. (2) The bhavcopy close canary at 18:52 sits seven
minutes after an ASYNCHRONOUS 18:45 submit against a publish time measured at 17:52 / 17:59 / 18:47 /
19:31 — and its skip is permanent, because by the next firing that session is no longer today. (3)
Past-expiry recovery at 08:52 shares the DEFAULT pool with the 15-second bracket sweep, so an overrun
past 09:15 stalls stop-loss evaluation. A fourth, rated Major, is the same shape: reconciliation at
08:50 was only *starting* after the 08:35 catch-up, never waiting for it. **The rule: when you retime
a job, enumerate what its old time was silently supplying — a settled day, a landed file, an idle
pool, a finished predecessor — and re-express each one explicitly.** The fix for (3) and (4) was one
line: both onto the catch-up's size-1 pool, which supplies ordering *and* isolation. Note (1) is a
pure N44/#14 — an armed check whose operand is structurally empty — reached by a one-line cron edit,
which is how cheaply that failure mode can be created.

**N66 · A BACKGROUND TASK CAN REPORT "completed (exit code 0)" WHEN THE COMMAND DIED BEFORE IT
STARTED.** 2026-08-12, measured: a cross-vendor review round was launched with its output redirected
to `"$SCR/rev2.out"`, but `SCR` was unset in that shell, so bash failed the redirect
(`/rev2.out: Permission denied`) and killed the line before Codex ran. The task notification still
said **completed, exit code 0**, and the review state file was 3.5 hours stale — the round simply had
not happened. The tell is the DURATION: an `xhigh` review of a 1,500-line diff returning in two
minutes is not a fast review, it is no review. Same family as the `mvnw | Out-File` pipeline-exit
trap: **read the artifact's mtime, never the launcher's exit code.** Cheap habit: `ls -l` the state
file and check its timestamp moved before reading its contents as fresh.

**N65 · CHECK WHETHER THE DEFECT ACTUALLY FIRED BEFORE CLAIMING URGENCY.** 2026-08-12: the swing
entry-forfeiture fix was written, reviewed and framed as closing a LIVE money-path hole — the
catch-up is armed (`ARTHA_SWING_CATCHUP_ENABLED=true`, verified by `docker inspect`), so the path
was genuinely reachable. Then the morning's scheduled verification showed the first complete
two-phase cycle ran with `entriesReady` TRUE and **no screen-mismatch, arming-unknown or deadline
skip firing at all**; the zero entries were every candidate refused by an ADMISSION rail (the 6%
portfolio open-risk cap, the 12/12 book governor). So the fix is PREVENTIVE, not stanching a bleed.
Reachable is not the same as firing, and armed is not the same as exercised. **Both belong in the
verdict sentence, because the difference decides whether something deploys tonight or tomorrow.**
Related: the same run disproved the previous day's "entries permanently broken" conclusion for the
second time — see N60.

**N63 · "WAS IT MERGED?" IS THE WRONG QUESTION. ASK WHAT HAPPENED TO IT IN REVIEW.** 2026-08-11,
hours after writing N62 and while acting on it. Having established that `ae2ca115` ("poll the evening
chain from 18:00") was not in `main`, the Architect concluded it was LOST WORK, rebuilt the schedule
PR on its polling design, and shipped a commit describing the design as "recovered". It was not lost.
It is an INTERMEDIATE commit on the branch that became #1333, and the review state still sitting in
`.claude/skills/codex-code-review/state/evening-chain.*` shows an **8-round cross-vendor review that
took the polling apart and only reached APPROVED after every poll was DELETED** -- six Majors, all
consequences of polling the alerting and observability jobs, one of which ("early ingestion polls can
manufacture false-green evidence") the Architect then re-derived from scratch and documented as an
ACCEPTED COST in the very commit that reintroduced it. Another ("bhavcopy canary re-pages on every
poll") was rediscovered independently an hour later, and it was that rediscovery -- not the merge
check -- that finally surfaced the prior review. **A commit that is absent from `main` has at least
three very different explanations: never finished, lost, or REJECTED. The merge check cannot tell
them apart, and the rejected case is the one where resurrecting it does damage.** The evidence is
cheap and local: `.claude/skills/codex-*/state/` retains every review thread, verbatim, indefinitely.
Rule: before reviving any unmerged commit, grep the review state for its branch or target name.
N62 said resolve the SHA; N63 says resolve its FATE.

**N62 · A COMMIT SUBJECT IS NOT EVIDENCE THE WORK IS IN MAIN -- AND AN ORPHANED COMMIT READS
EXACTLY LIKE A MERGED ONE.** 2026-08-11: the Architect asserted four times, to the owner, that "#1333
changed the evening chain to poll from 18:00, deployed this morning". Every word of the subject line
was real; none of the work was in `main`. **`989d63e8` (the actual #1333 squash) is *settle the swing
book at 16:00, take entries at 08:35*.** The polling commit is `ae2ca115` -- present in the local
object store, visible at the top of `git log` in one checkout, and belonging to **no branch at all**
(`git branch -a --contains` returns empty; it is an ancestor of neither `origin/main` nor `HEAD`).
A pruned branch leaves its commits reachable through the reflog for weeks, so they keep appearing in
casual `git log` output long after the work is gone. The tell that was available and unused: the
18:55 check found the 19:xx cron literals INTACT in `main`, and that was rationalised as "vestigial
literals under a poll" rather than read as "the poll is not here". **Rule: before citing a PR number
as shipped, resolve the SHA -- `git log origin/main --grep`, then `git merge-base --is-ancestor
<sha> origin/main`. A subject line proves someone wrote it, never that it landed.** Cost: four wrong
statements to the owner over a day, plus a schedule PR built on the wrong design (single-shot cron
shift) that had to be rebuilt on the orphan's own argument. The compensating mercy is that the
orphan's reasoning was recovered before merge, not after a lost trading session.

**N60 · "NO CALLER PASSES TRUE" IS NOT PROOF A PATH IS DEAD -- READ THE GATE THAT ADMITS WORK TO IT.**
2026-08-11: the Architect concluded swing ENTRIES could never run, from three correct observations --
both 16:00 schedulers pass `entriesEnabled=false`, the 08:35 job is a catch-up that only replays
PENDING sessions, and the day produced zero entry runs -- and told the owner the swing book had
stopped taking positions. A plan was written and a cross-vendor plan review consumed before the
disconfirming code was read. **`SwingCatchUpStateRepository.seedMissing` seeds a session precisely
WHEN its run row has `entries_enabled` false:** `WHERE NOT EXISTS (... AND COALESCE(entries_enabled,
true))`. The settle's own row is what MAKES the session eligible next morning; the design works end to
end, confirmed against live rows before the wrong fix shipped. The disconfirming evidence had already
been quoted OUT LOUD -- the upsert comment says *"the 16:00 exits pass writes false; the 08:35
catch-up then writes true for the SAME (batch, run_date)"* -- read as INTENT when it was BEHAVIOUR.
**Rule: before declaring a path unreachable, find the predicate that admits work to it and evaluate
that predicate against live data.** Sibling of N59 (verify the consumer); this is verify the GATE.
Third premise error in one day.

**N61 · `docker compose up -d` CAN LEAVE A CONTAINER `Created` AND NEVER START IT -- exit code 0, no
logs, no error.** 2026-08-11, measured: image created 18:00:13 IST, container created 18:00:25, and
`up -d` returned 0 with the container sitting in state `Created`. A plain `docker start` booted it
38 s later (`StartedAt` 18:01:03) and the app came up in 5.1 s. Between those two moments the signal
engine was DOWN with every command in the deploy chain reporting success. CLAUDE.md already warns
that *"plain `up -d` STARTS an already-Created container instead of replacing it"*; this is the worse
sibling -- **it can leave it Created and not start it at all.** **A deploy must end in a jar
FINGERPRINT plus a `docker ps` RUNNING check, and must fail loudly on either -- never on exit codes.**

⚠️ **CORRECTION, same day, before this note was merged.** The first draft blamed the 17:15 scheduled
deploy task: "it fired and deployed nothing". That is NOT supported by the timestamps. The 18:00:13
image and 18:00:25 container are the ARCHITECT's own manual build and compose, run after measuring
(correctly, at 17:55) that the running jar still lacked the #1340 marker and the container env still
read `upstox`. The scheduled task ran LONG, past that manual deploy, and its ledger PR ([#1349])
describes the correct end state -- including the transient `2 loaded / 36 unresolved` reload row at
18:00:24 that self-healed at 18:01:46, 82 s later -- because by the time it verified, the manual
deploy had landed. **It recorded someone else's deploy as its own, and nothing in its receipts could
have revealed that.** Two lessons worth more than the original claim: a long-running deploy task and
a human deploying the same services CONCURRENTLY produce a receipt that is true and misattributed;
and the Architect asserted "the routine deployed nothing" from a 17:55 measurement plus an absence of
artifacts, without checking `StartedAt`/`.Created` -- the timestamps that settle it in one command.
Fourth premise error of the day, and the only one caught by re-reading its own PR.

**N55 · A SUSPICIOUS NUMBER MAY ALREADY HAVE A MERGED FIX — GREP THE METRIC'S OWN NAME BEFORE
MEASURING THE SYSTEM THAT PRODUCES IT.** Ledger row H10 (`candidates = 0` on 5 of 6 minervini runs)
was investigated as a live funnel defect for three read-only passes — per-gate screen counts, the
setups table, a live funnel call, all healthy — before anyone read what the zero column is populated
from. It was a REPORTING defect, diagnosed and fixed the previous morning in
[#1324](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1324), whose commit subject literally
names the symptom. A merged-but-undeployed fix produces exactly the same live evidence as an unfixed
defect, so every measurement of the running system confirms the symptom and says nothing about
whether it is solved. ⚠️ **And the disproof was in the FIRST query all along:** `would_enter` read
17–21 on the very same rows, and the admission probe walks the SAME list the counter reports — so the
list demonstrably held ≥21 entries and the counter could only be lying. **Before believing a number,
look for another value in the same row that contradicts it.** Memory:
`check-for-a-recent-fix-before-diagnosing`.

**N56 · IDENTITY IS NOT PROOF — and the fix for that class is usually SEMANTIC, not syntactic.**
`git-prune-merged.sh`'s GitHub fallback had never once fired (25 merged branches kept): it required
the merged PR's `headRefOid` to equal the local tip, but `main` is `strict: true`, so a green PR is
routinely brought forward with a server-side `update-branch` whose merge commit the local checkout
never fetches. Matching the tip against the PR's **commit list** fixes that — and, alone, is a false
DELETE, because an INTERMEDIATE commit of a merged PR matches the list just as well as the final one
and its tree is not what landed. **Eleven local branches were in exactly that state.** So the two
questions must be asked separately: IDENTITY (is this PR about this branch?) and PROOF (did this
tip's content land?), the latter as a path-scoped diff against that PR's own `mergeCommit` — never
against current main, whose later commits are what made the cheap test inconclusive in the first
place. ⚠️ Review then raised a Critical about malformed rows; the prescribed fix (field validation)
did NOT close it — a row whose `mergeCommit` equals the tip passes every syntactic check and makes
the proof compare the branch against itself. The guard that works is semantic: a squash commit is by
definition a commit ON main (`merge-base --is-ancestor`).

**N57 · A FIFTH RED-PROOF SHAPE: the TEST DOUBLE disarms the proof.** Distinct from N54 — the
mutation lands, the assertion is capable, the code is wrong, and the suite stays green because the
stub answers identically for both versions. The `gh` double diverged from the real command **five
times** in one PR series: ignored `--json`; emitted one OID per line where the tool emits one row per
PR; exited 1 instead of 0 on a no-match (making "no merged PR" and "the API refused" the same signal
— the exact conflation the production fix was about); omitted a newly-added field and shifted every
positional read; and accepted any `--jq` containing `@tsv` while independently emitting the expected
field order, so REORDERING production columns stayed green. **A red-proof tests THREE claims: the
code changed, the test noticed, and the double can tell the two versions apart.** Cure: the stub
validates the ENTIRE invocation — subcommand, `--state`, exact `--json` list, exact `--jq` string.
Two further proofs in the same series reddened and proved nothing: one crashed under `set -u`
(nine cases red, none naming an assertion), one tripped an unrelated exact-string pin. **`passed N,
failed M` is not a verdict — the failing case NAMES must match the change.**

**N58 · `gh pr list` IS GRAPHQL, AND THAT BUDGET IS SEPARATE FROM REST CORE.** Measured:
`gh api rate_limit --jq .resources.core` read **4993/5000** — "the rate limit is fine" by the obvious
check — while every `gh pr list` failed `API rate limit already exceeded`. A prune run stopped
part-way through and its output read exactly like "nothing left to prune". Any tool that pipes `gh`
into `while ... done < <(...)` discards the exit status and cannot tell a refusal from an empty
answer. Check `.resources.graphql`, and always read the command's exit code.

**N48 · ⚠️ THE CANARY ALLOWLIST COULD NOT BE SET, AND NOTHING WOULD HAVE SAID SO.**
`CorporateActionJob` has read `artha.corporate-actions.symbols` since it was written. The key existed
in **no** `application.yml` and had **no** compose passthrough. Putting
`ARTHA_CORPORATE_ACTIONS_SYMBOLS` in `.env` would have produced no error, no log line and no effect —
and the sweep would have taken all **248 DETECTED** symbols instead of the 2 the owner approved, on
the path whose remediation OOM-crashed live Postgres three times.

Found only because arming it was an actual task. **Both halves are required and exactly one was
missing, which is what made it invisible:** a compose passthrough alone delivers the variable to the
container and it is never read — and `docker inspect` shows it set, which is precisely the check an
operator runs to confirm. `CorporateActionKnobPassthroughTest` now guards both sides for three knobs,
**deriving** the env name from the property rather than writing it twice.

Picked for unambiguity: **ITC** 7/7 anchors diverged at ratio 1.0426 over 4,970 daily bars, **HDFCBANK**
a textbook 1.99992 split over 4,970. Verified by BEHAVIOUR, not config: six `ay_corporate_action_*`
metrics are now exported, and they exist only when the `@ConditionalOnProperty` bean loads.
`anchor_noise_total` and `unadjusted_tail_bars_total` both 0.0 — nothing has run yet.

**N49 · Five consecutive review rounds on #1333, and I introduced three of the five Criticals while
fixing the previous one.** Ledger-worthy because it is a *pattern*, not five accidents:

| Round | Critical | Whose |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | live scheduler bypassed `doctrine.enabled()` — a disabled family could exit and stamp completion | mine (first cut) |
| 2 | the seed gate suppressed every exits-only session, one layer above where I had fixed it | pre-existing |
| 3 | hourly intent ticks made 09:05 permanently override the authoritative 16:00 arming | **mine, fixing round 2** |
| 4 | the overwrite covered only a SUCCESSFUL settle — and the settle's write is fail-soft | **mine, fixing round 3** |
| 5 | the recovery pass wrote the marker that authorised its own next entry | **mine, fixing round 4** |

The through-line: **each fix added a second writer, a second source of truth, or a second input to a
computed threshold, and inherited the semantics the single-writer version had.** The question that
would have caught all three — *what NEW state does this create, and which existing consumer reads it
as if it were the old state?* Full write-up: memory `a-fix-can-reopen-the-hole-it-closed` §5.

The sibling did it in miniature: #1283's `DEPTH_SLACK = 2` widened the probe footprint correctly and
widened the materiality DENOMINATOR with it, so one hole in a depth-20 strategy stopped being
material — silently undoing the 21→22 recalibration made earlier in the same PR. **Retreated to 0**
with the gap documented, on the rule that *a measured-correct gate beats an unmeasured wider one.*

**N50 · A migration that redefines an EXISTING writer's semantics reaches every fixture that used
it — and those fixtures do not fail, they change meaning.** V061 made `recordScheduled` provisional.
`SwingPaperExitCriticalsIntegrationTest` seeded intent through it, so three of its assertions
silently began exercising the new exits-only path instead of what they were written for. They still
passed. Caught only by a full-suite verify. **When a migration changes what an existing method
MEANS, grep every caller including tests, and ask of each whether it meant the old thing.**

**N51 · Two catalogued method traps, walked into anyway, in one session.** (a) `-Dtest='A+B'` — the
invalid filter form — reported **12 passing tests off a STALE surefire report** for a class that
actually has 14; the comma form ran 18. (b) A red-proof stayed GREEN because the test drove
`warm()`, which calls `reload()` unconditionally and therefore **cannot observe the field under
test**. Both are in `CLAUDE.md` and `MEMORY.md` already. **Knowing a trap and having a habit that
avoids it are different things** — the only defence that worked both times was re-reading the
failure text rather than the exit code.

#### 2026-08-10 (late) — the swing day gets SPLIT: exits at 16:00, entries at 08:35

**N46 · Owner decision: close the book as soon as the session closes.** Exits move from 20:00/20:05
to **16:00/16:02 IST**; entries move to the **08:35** morning pass. The split is possible because the
two halves need different data and only one needs the bhavcopy.

`computed` 2026-08-07, for the symbols held in the two swing books: **11 of 15** daily bars were
written by **`KITE` at 20:00:47–20:05:33** — fetched by the batch itself, on demand, through the
cache-first path — against **4 from `BHAVCOPY` at 19:30`**. A stop on a held name needs that name's
own bar and nothing else. The **screen** needs the whole ~1800-symbol NSE bhavcopy, which landed at
17:52 / 18:41 / 18:48 / 19:08 / 19:30+ across the last 11 sessions — and our own history **cannot
bound that tighter**, because the fetch cron ran at 19:30 and masked every earlier availability.

**N46b · `SwingBatchCatchUp` was already built, already armed, and had never once worked.** Its own
doctrine says the quiet part: *between a session's 15:30 close and the next 09:15 open, that
session's bar is final, so a run in that window reads exactly the bar the on-time run would have.*
`ARTHA_SWING_CATCHUP_ENABLED=true` live. And the only two rows ever written to
`swing_catchup_runs` are 2026-07-17, **both `ABANDONED / NO_SCHEDULE_INTENT`** — it refused for want
of a schedule-intent row that nothing wrote. Recording that intent is what makes the morning path
real.

**N46c · The marker hole, and it is N44's shape again — caught in design, before code.** The
catch-up skips any session present in `swing_batch_runs`. An exits-only 16:00 run writes such a row,
*correctly*, because it did evaluate every held stop — which is what the 08:30 canary and 20:15
heartbeat ask about. Left alone it would have skipped **every** session and the book would never have
taken another entry. **V060** adds `entries_enabled`: the catch-up asks `hasRunWithEntries`, the
canary and heartbeat keep asking `hasRun`, the upsert ORs so a later exits-only re-run cannot re-open
a session already entered, and `NULL` (pre-V060) reads as TRUE so the deploy does not hand the
catch-up every historical session at once.

**N46d · Two Criticals in the first cut, both money-path, both from reusing the wrong overload.** The
snapshot-carrying `runAndRecord` overloads hardcode `executionArmed = true` for the catch-up's
benefit (a historical session's arming may legitimately have changed since). Calling one from the
LIVE scheduler bypassed `doctrine.enabled()` entirely — **a disabled strategy could exit and stamp
completion** — and also dropped `runScheduled`'s FAILED-alert envelope, so a settle that threw would
have died as a lone log line with every held stop unevaluated. Both found by cross-vendor review
before merge. Five of the same round's eight Majors were consequences of *polling the alerting jobs*
and are gone by construction: those crons are back to single-shot, and only bhavcopy + NSE EOD still
poll.

**N46e · ⚠️ OPEN — the closing auction is unverified on this path.** Since 2026-08-03 continuous
trading in Category-I stocks ends 15:15 and a **15:15–15:30 call auction sets the official close**.
Whether Kite's *daily candle* carries that auction close by 16:00, or still the 15:15 continuous
price, is **not measured**. Flagged to the owner, who chose to arm 16:00 immediately rather than
shadow a session first. **First live settle must be compared symbol-by-symbol against that evening's
bhavcopy close** — a wrong close here is silent and perfectly plausible-looking.

**N47 · H4 answered: the 18 stale swing positions are legitimately held, not stranded.** `computed`
2026-08-10: `exit_skipped = 0` on **every** `swing_batch_runs` row for both books over the last two
weeks, and exits DID fire when stops were hit (manas `TRAILING_STOP` 08-06; manas + minervini one
each 07-31). All 18 open rows carry a non-null `stop_loss`. The exit path evaluates them every
session; the stops simply have not been hit. **No cleanup action** — the owner's "measure first"
call was the right one, and it turned a proposed square-off into a no-op.

- ⚠️ **A false finding I nearly filed from the same data:** `open_at_start = 15` against **12** open
  minervini rows looked like a week-stable counter defect. It is not — `openAtStart` counts open
  **signal ANCHORS**, not paper positions, and `SwingBatchEngine`'s own comment at the entry pass
  says so outright ("this pass classifies held purely from active signal anchors … so a symbol with
  an open position but no live anchor reads as fresh here"). The distinguishing check was reading the
  code, not re-running the query.
- **Genuinely open, unexplained:** minervini reported `candidates = 0` on **5 of the last 6
  sessions** (08-04/05/06/07/10) while manas ran 93–111 each, and the screen itself produced ~1,780
  rows every one of those days. The funnel between screen and batch is emptying. Not diagnosed.

    opened, one entry went in, the sixth candidate hit the wall. The book re-saturated at 6 within a
    single batch. Nothing about the signals changed — 105 candidates against 100/103/111 on the
    preceding sessions.

    converts to entries **one-for-one and immediately**, so the two levers are not equivalent —
    ageing out N stale positions buys exactly N entries, whereas re-sizing the cap changes the
    steady-state ceiling. **Both are still owner calls; neither is started.**

- **N37 · ⚠️ RETRACTED 2026-08-08, one day after merging — the central causal claim is WRONG. See
  N38.** The three phantom anchors are real, but they hold **ZERO slots** and clearing them frees

- ~~**N37 · minervini's silent refusal is SOLVED — three phantom anchors from 2026-07-03 are holding
  slots, and the book reads 15/12.**~~ Found by running the §0 seven-location enumeration, not by
  looking at N26. **H4 predicted this exact residue and nobody connected it to the entry starvation.**
  - **Measured, all this session:**
    - `strategy.risk_settings` → `minervini / max_open_paper_positions = {"value": 12, "enabled": true}` (**sourced**)
    - `paper_positions WHERE book='minervini' AND status='OPEN'` = **12** (**computed**)
    - `signals WHERE status='TAKEN' AND signal_type='ENTRY' AND book='minervini'` = **15** (**computed**)
    - the three with no open position: **signal 20 `SENORES`, 23 `TMB`, 26 `INDUSINDBK`, all generated
      2026-07-03** (**computed**) — exactly the residue H4's closeout spun out and left as owner-tier
    - the batch's `open_at_start` reads **15**, i.e. it counts **TAKEN anchors, not open positions**
  - **So the book presents as 15 against a cap of 12 — three OVER — and refuses every candidate.**
    The phantoms can never resolve on their own: there is no position to exit, so nothing will ever
    close them. **This is a permanent, self-sustaining block, not a transient saturation.**
  - **It explains the 08-07 asymmetry precisely.** manas-arora sat at 5 against 6, one close freed a
    slot, and the next batch admitted one (N36). minervini would need **four** closes to admit one —
    three to work off the phantom overhang, then one for an actual slot. That is why five sessions of
    closes produced nothing.
  - **This makes the N26 lever much cheaper than "re-size the cap".** Clearing three stale 2026-07-03
    signal rows restores three slots immediately. **Still owner-tier** — it mutates signal status on a
    money-adjacent path, and the correct terminal status (EXPIRED? CANCELLED? a new state?) is a
    doctrine call, not a cleanup detail. **Not started; nothing was mutated by this investigation.**

    intended (it is what makes a phantom cost a slot) or is itself the defect. Settle that before
    choosing between "clear the rows" and "change the counter" — they are different fixes with
    different blast radii. → **That doubt is what N38 resolved, and resolving it falsified the row.**

- **N38 · ⚠️ N37 IS WRONG, AND THE REAL FINDING IS A LIVE ONE-CLICK DEFECT.** Investigated 2026-08-08
  (owner chose "investigate first, don't mutate" — correctly, as it turns out: the mutation N37
  proposed would have achieved nothing).
  - **Correction 1 — the phantoms hold ZERO slots.** The gate is `RiskService.entryVeto:412-415`
    → `positions.openCount(book) >= max_open`, and `PaperPositionRepository.openCount:674-681` is
    `SELECT count(*) FROM paper_positions WHERE status='OPEN' AND book=?` (**sourced**, both read
    directly). minervini holds **12 real OPEN positions against a cap of 12** — saturated on
    positions alone. `open_at_start` (15) comes from `SwingBatchEngine.computeAdmissionProbe:629-630`
    over `signals.activeEntries()`, and its own javadoc (`:575-577`) says it is
    **"measurement-only… it NEVER changes admission"**. **So minervini needs ONE close to admit one,
    exactly like manas-arora — not four.** N37's headline arithmetic was wrong.
  - **Correction 2 — "they can never self-resolve" is refuted by their own siblings.**
    `SwingBatchEngine.emitExit:1053` transitions the anchor to `EXPIRED` with no reference to any
    paper position. Two of the same stranded set already did exactly that: signal 24 `MANAKSTEEL`
    (exit sig 95, 07-27) and signal 27 `PNBHOUSING` (exit sig 125, 07-30, effect row records
    `decision=SKIPPED, target_position_ids={}` — no position to close). The remaining three are
    simply still above their trail (`sell_decisions` 08-07: SENORES 1340.10 vs 1295.63, TMB 872.80
    vs 787.85, INDUSINDBK 1020.20 vs 968.09, all HOLD).
  - **Correction 3 — the refusal is silent BY DESIGN, not because of phantoms.** `entryVeto`'s
    javadoc (`:404-405`): *"the kill-switch and max-open rails write no audit"*. No `risk_audit` row,
    no log line. That is the whole explanation for N26's "silent" half.
  - **ROOT CAUSE of the phantoms:** on **2026-07-05 14:02:34 IST** the `V021__paper_books` migration
    ran `DELETE FROM paper_orders; DELETE FROM paper_positions;` (owner-approved "every book starts
    flat", commit `738b43c9`) and **did not touch `strategy.signals`**, stranding the 8 minervini
    `TAKEN` anchors emitted the evening before. Fingerprint: `paper_positions` and `paper_orders`
    both have `min(id)=10` with no gaps after, first surviving row 2h46m after V021 ran, and
    `paper_account` ids 2-5 share that exact insert timestamp (**computed**).
  - ⚠️ **THE PART THAT MATTERS — IT CAN RECUR TODAY, WITH A BUTTON.** `POST /api/v1/paper/reset` is
    V021's DELETE with a UI on it: `PaperService.reset:1673-1681` is `positions.deleteAll(book)` +
    `orders.deleteAll(book)` and **nothing else** — no signal-status reset, no effect-ledger cleanup
    (**sourced**, read directly). `PaperController:335-341`, wired at
    `frontend-react/src/pages/paper/PaperPage.tsx:261-273`. **Resetting the minervini ledger right now
    would instantly strand 12 fresh phantom anchors.** No guard exists and no PR has ever addressed
    it. **This is the real deliverable of the investigation** — a latent live defect, found only
    because the owner said investigate rather than mutate.
  - **Adjacent, narrower:** the runtime CAS→open-failure window is closed for SWING books by the
    durable effect ledger (`AutoPaperListener:76-80` writes `requireEntry` before the CAS;
    `SwingBatchCatchUp:473-494` replays it), landed 2026-07-27 via V050/V051 — but `isSwingBook` is
    false for **scalper and manual** books, so that window is still open there. Zero residue today
    (0 TAKEN scalper signals), so it is latent, not active.
  - **Real cost of the 3 phantoms, correctly scoped:** they read as "held" in
    `SwingBatchEngine.openLotsBySymbol:562-570`, so a fresh entry on those three names routes to the
    pyramid path and is skipped, and `open_at_start` over-reports by 3. **They do not consume
    slot-cap headroom.**
  - **Falsifiable prediction, cheap to check:** the next time a minervini position closes, the
    following batch should admit an entry at 11/12. If it does not, the positions-based reading is
    wrong and this correction needs re-opening.
  - **Open doubt carried:** `signals` `min(id)=20` and no code path in the repo deletes signals —

| task_2560273c | 2026-07-11 | Funnel-knob tunability (EVO §13 row 21 third family): funnel props (rs-min etc.) are service-global config, no per-strategy YAML leaf exists — needs a config leaf + resolver plumb before EVO can tune them (the design's "funnel knobs wait"). | **CLOSED MIS-SCOPED 2026-07-17 (BRIEF-INVALID at the builder gate; zero code written — do NOT rebuild from this row).** The chip's prescription ("a config leaf + resolver plumb") is necessary but nowhere near sufficient: it would ship a knob that is **inert across its whole useful range, then fatal beyond it**. (1) **Not sweepable:** `ManasFunnelService:92` orders `rs_rank DESC`, `rs_min` trims the BOTTOM (`:88`), and `BacktestRunner:728` replays `pinned.get(0)` — the TOP pick. (#706 documented the static-universe divergence as "per-name expectancy only" — that does not make it safe to TUNE on.) (3) **"One resolver, both call sites" is unsatisfiable by design** — `UniverseResolver:67-68` javadoc: the pin is "a display/validation snapshot, **never the authoritative evaluation universe**"; live re-reads per session (`SwingBatchEngine:155-159`). (4) **Scope error in the source design:** `liquidity-multiple` (named alongside rs-min at EVO §13:686) is NOT a funnel prop — it is applied at screen-WRITE time (`ManasScreenService:59,71`) and baked into `passes_all`; unreachable per-strategy at read time. **Architect-measured, and decisive:** `marketdata.manas_arora_screen_results` holds **10 distinct screen_dates, 2026-07-03 → 2026-07-16** — two weeks. A point-in-time funnel has NO data on any real backtest window. **The real work is an OWNER/design item, not a chip:** historical screen backfill (bhavcopy + geometry recompute per date) + an `asOf`-threaded resolver (the endpoint already accepts `asOf`) + a runner that REPLAYS the funnel rather than `get(0)`. That touches the swing batch, the job pipeline and the screen data lineage — it belongs back in the EVO design before anyone builds it. Bonus defect found + chipped: `universe.bucket` is already a live/sim divergence (task_8f139394). |
| task_1b85c64f | 2026-07-10 | Backtest 1h bars anchor on the hour; live 1h candles anchor 09:15+n·60m → a 30-min phase gap between sim and live 1h series (FID finding). Decide + align one side. | **DONE — MERGED [#1033](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1033) 2026-07-26** (HOLD tier, owner-approved before merge: it changes every 1h backtest number). ⚠️ **THE CHIP TEXT WAS INVERTED — corrected at source 2026-07-26.** It is the BACKTEST that anchors **:30 IST** and LIVE that anchors **:00 IST**, not the other way round. Sim: `TickwiseGoldenRunner.bucketFloor` floored the RAW UTC epoch, and IST is UTC+05:30, so a UTC-hour boundary lands at :30 IST. Live: `V029__candles_1h_ist_reanchor.sql` moved `candles_1h` to `time_bucket(INTERVAL '1 hour', bucket, 'Asia/Kolkata')` on 2026-07-04 — and that migration's OWN comment records the pre-fix behaviour as splitting "at :30 IST", i.e. the identical defect, fixed on the live side only and never mirrored into the sim. **Owner decision 2026-07-26: move the SIM to match live** (live is the side that trades and the cagg is already correct). **BUILT — HOLD tier, PR left OPEN.** Scoped to intra-day intervals: the IST offset is 19800 s, which divides 3m (÷180=110), 5m (÷300=66) and 15m (÷900=22) EXACTLY, so the shift is a pure no-op there; only 1h leaves a remainder (19800 mod 3600 = 1800) — precisely the 30-minute phase error. **1d deliberately left on the raw epoch floor**: re-anchoring it changes only the bar LABEL (a 05:30-IST day and a 00:00-IST day both wholly contain one 09:15–15:30 session), but goldens compare byte-strings, so it would rewrite the frozen vectors of 8 strategies on a 1d primary for zero behavioural gain. | task_db8bdf1e | 2026-07-25 | `main` required-status-checks were unsatisfiable, so EVERY PR sat `BLOCKED` and every merge used `--admin` — which bypasses ALL required checks and would hide a genuinely-red one. **TWO causes, found while merging #1003/#1006.** (1) `frontend` named a job that no workflow emits any more (it is `react`) — provably dead on every PR. **FIXED 2026-07-25: removed from the context list** (`PATCH .../required_status_checks`; before = `[contracts, e2e, frontend, gitleaks, build-test (market-data), build-test (backtest), build-test (strategy-gateway)]`, after = the same minus `frontend`; `strict:true` and `enforce_admins:false` unchanged). ⚠️ NOTE the obvious-looking fix `frontend`→`react` is WRONG — see (2). (2) **STILL OPEN — the structural half:** 5 of the 6 remaining required contexts come from PATH-FILTERED workflows (`contracts` from ci-contracts.yml, `gitleaks` + `build-test (×3)` from ci-java.yml), so they never report on a PR that touches none of those paths and such a PR is blocked forever — proven on docs-only #1006, where ONLY `e2e` + `verdict` ran. `e2e` (ci-e2e.yml) is the only required context that runs on every PR; `react` is path-filtered too, which is why swapping the name in would rebuild the same trap. Fix needs a design call with a COST tradeoff (owner-facing): drop the path filters (every docs PR then pays the ~8 m market-data shard — CLAUDE.md calls that shard the PR floor), or add always-reporting shims / a change-detection `changes` job + `if:`-gated heavy jobs reporting one stable check name (watch the skipped-job-vs-required-check semantics and the double-report race when `paths` and `paths-ignore` both match a mixed PR). Until then: code-touching PRs should merge normally; docs-only PRs still need `--admin`. **STRUCTURAL HALF DONE 2026-07-26 — owner picked the change-detection option.** The `paths:` filters are GONE from ci-java.yml + ci-contracts.yml; both workflows now always trigger, a cheap `changes` job classifies the diff (merge base on a PR, `github.event.before` on a main push) and the heavy jobs carry `if: needs.changes.outputs.* == 'true'`. **The load-bearing fact, sourced from GitHub's docs, not assumed:** "If a job within a workflow is skipped due to a conditional, it will report its status as `Success`" whereas "if a workflow is skipped due to path filtering [...] checks associated with that workflow will remain in a `Pending` state" and block the PR — the two skip mechanisms are NOT interchangeable, which is exactly why the old filter was unsatisfiable. Job NAMES are unchanged, so all six required contexts keep resolving. **`gitleaks` is deliberately left UNGATED** (a secret can land in any file type; a docs-only PR is precisely when you still want the scan, and it costs ~30 s). Classifier is **fail-safe = RUN**: an unresolvable base (force-push, first push, unanticipated event shape) runs the full suite rather than declaring nothing changed. Cost answer to the row's own tradeoff: a docs-only PR now pays ~30 s of gitleaks instead of the ~8 m market-data shard, and needs no `--admin`. ✅ **THIRD CAUSE RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (owner ruled it ACCIDENTAL).** `lock_branch` set to `false` via a full-object `PUT .../branches/main/protection`. The payload was built by mirroring the live GET field-by-field, and a before/after diff confirmed **`lock_branch` was the ONLY setting that moved** — all six required contexts, `strict: true`, `enforce_admins: false`, `required_linear_history`, `required_conversation_resolution`, `allow_force_pushes/deletions` and `restrictions` all byte-identical (the protection API replaces the WHOLE object, so a sloppy PUT silently weakens other controls — always diff after). Also confirmed there are **no repository rulesets** (`GET /rulesets` returns `[]`), so classic protection was the only mechanism in play and the diagnosis was complete. **Why it read as accidental:** with `enforce_admins: false` the lock stopped nobody — the owner bypassed it trivially and is the only person who can merge — while defeating the entire six-context required-check setup on every merge. Superseded note, kept for the record: ⚠️ **THIRD CAUSE, found while merging #1030 — `main` has `lock_branch: {"enabled": true}`, i.e. the branch is READ-ONLY.** With `enforce_admins: false`, that means EVERY merge must use `--admin`, which bypasses ALL required checks including a genuinely red one — so the "no more `--admin`" outcome this row is really about is STILL NOT DELIVERED, and neither the dead-context fix nor the path-filter fix could ever have delivered it alone. Proof: #1030 had all six required contexts reporting SUCCESS and `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, yet a plain `gh pr merge --squash` returned `the base branch policy prohibits the merge` with `mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED`; it merged only with `--admin`. Owner decision chipped as `task_7bea266e`: was the lock deliberate? If accidental, disabling it is what makes #1030 pay off; if deliberate, record it in CLAUDE.md so no future session re-files this as a bug, and accept that required checks are advisory. **Do not read this row as "docs-only PRs now merge without `--admin`" — they do not.** ✅ **SKIP PATH PROVEN 2026-07-26 on [#1032](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1032)** (docs-only, based on the merged main): `build-test (strategy-gateway)` **2 s**, `(backtest)` **4 s**, `(market-data)` **6 s** — all three materialised and PASSED rather than never reporting (market-data alone was the ~8-minute PR floor before) — `contracts` **5 s**, and `gitleaks` still ran ungated at **9 s** exactly as designed. `e2e` is unchanged because it was never path-filtered. Superseded note, kept for the record: ⚠️ **NOT YET PROVEN ON THE SKIP PATH:** the PR that ships this change edits both workflow files, which are themselves in the classifier's path set, so it runs everything and exercises only the `true` branch. The `false` branch (shards report Success-by-skip, PR mergeable without `--admin`) must be observed on the next genuinely docs-only PR before this row is trustworthy. | **CLOSED 2026-07-26 — all THREE causes fixed; a plain `gh pr merge --squash` now works** |

| task_0bed1621 | 2026-07-26 | **Expose `SignalEngine.lastBarReceivedAtMs` + `lastBarEvaluatedAtMs` as an operator-readable surface** (Micrometer gauges on the existing 8082 actuator, and/or a small typed endpoint). These two are the ONLY unconfounded engine-liveness oracles, and NOTHING outside the process can read them today. Consequence, established across 4 cross-vendor review rounds on the canary retirement: **on a fully quiet session, engine liveness is currently UNPROVABLE by an operator or agent.** Every proxy admits a false PASS — rejections are direction-dependent; `strategy.signals` mixes the swing BATCH engine; `subscriber_health_events` is write-only fail-soft so absence proves nothing; `signal_eval_outcomes` freshness only proves the ROLLUP thread (its own scheduler, never the eval thread) is alive; and a thread dump parked on `LinkedBlockingQueue.take()` cannot distinguish quiet tape from a dropped Redis listener, because candle RECEIPT is what submits the drain. SCOPE: read surface ONLY — no new detector in the same PR (one was just retired for keying on a confounded signal). Parity ladder applies (edits live `SignalEngine`): `GoldenDeterminismTest` lives in `libs/strategy-engine`, NOT backtest-service. | **DONE 2026-07-26** — two Micrometer gauges on strategy-signal's existing 8082 actuator: `ay_signal_bar_received_age_seconds` and `ay_signal_bar_evaluated_age_seconds`, both publishing an AGE IN SECONDS (directly comparable to a threshold; no clock-skew reconciliation between container and reader). Pre-registered at boot for the #895 reason — a lazily-created meter would leave a MISSING series indistinguishable from a dead engine, the same ambiguity one level up. **A NEGATIVE value is not a small age:** `-1` = no bar EVER received/evaluated on this boot (the stamps are boot-seeded for the canary's grace, so a plain age would read ~0 there and look identical to a fresh bar), and anything BELOW `-1` = the clock stepped backwards. Negatives are deliberately NOT clamped — an earlier draft floored them at 0, which hid a genuine clock fault behind a value that reads as freshly alive. **The operator verdict requires BOTH gauges:** received fresh + evaluated GROWING is an EVAL STALL, which a received-only reading (the first draft) would have called PASS. READ SURFACE ONLY, no detector (a separate owner decision). README §4.3 step 4 and both `session-analysis` SKILL.md copies now key the quiet-session verdict on the BOTH age gauges instead of the thread dump (received alone is not enough — bars can arrive while `signal-eval` is wedged), and the 'liveness is UNPROVABLE' language is gone — re-grepped, not assumed, after twice claiming it was gone while blocks still carried it. Parity ladder run and green (Golden 9/9, Parity 9/9) — though note it is structurally NOT exposed: the live `SignalEngine` has no class in `libs/strategy-engine` and is not on the golden replay path (`TickwiseGoldenRunner` is), so the gauges could not have moved a vector. |

At the observed D≈37s that put attempt 3 at ~144s — both faults (~73s, ~81s) cleared with 60-70s of margin. **Landing on attempt 3 is the EXPECTED outcome for any fault in (72s, 144s].** The REAL argument: only the `D→0` case is GUARANTEED (breaker OPEN ⇒ instant reject), and at 3 attempts that floor was `2×35s = 70s` — **SHORTER than both measured faults**. Sized against the FLOOR, not the distribution — which holds under either reading of the drill data, so the "measured distribution" this row asked for is not needed. |
| task_f10a02 | 2026-07-16 | **F10 chain can end WORSE than no retry (Opus review D2, CONFIRMED).** The chain's terminal state is unconditionally the LAST attempt's result — there is no "keep the best". Reachable shape: attempt 1 → `drops=20, loaded=19`; attempt 2 (+35s) → Kite regressed → `loaded=0`; attempt 3 → still bad → exhausts and returns, **engine frozen at 0 for the session where it would have been 19 without the retry**. Narrow trigger (needs monotonic degradation across 70s; the normal login trajectory improves as the breaker heals), bounded to one session, recovered by the 08:40 cron. Observed in-vitro during the 07-16 drill (boot 32 → attempt 1 = 0). Minimum useful fix: on the exhaustion path, compare `drops` against the first attempt's and say so in the `log.error` — that is the signal the retry actively harmed. Better: don't install a strictly-worse reload result. | **DONE — [#892](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/892) @ 27bd41fb, MERGED 2026-07-17 (deploy held for post-close, with task_f10a01).** Took the "better" option, then went further after 4 cross-vendor rounds. **ROOT CAUSE the chips only circled: `liveCandidates > 0 && loadedCount == 0` is a GAP INFERENCE, and a gap cannot distinguish "correctly loaded nothing" from "everything failed."** Health now counts failures EXPLICITLY (`unresolvedDrops == 0 && loadErrors == 0`); `loadedCount` is absent from health entirely — stricter adherence to "`unresolved == 0`, NEVER `loaded > 0`" than the first draft managed. That also closed the live hole where a reload whose strategies all took an UNCOUNTED skip reported "0 loaded, 0 drops" and logged **"resolved every universe" over a DEAD engine**. Keep-best is **per-strategy**: own-failure ⇒ last-good retained AT ITS REGISTRY-WALK POSITION; RESOLVED_EMPTY stand-aside ⇒ always installed; absent/disabled ⇒ dropped (structural, never inferred — they `continue` before the retain call). `start()` now takes the boot outcome and arms on `!boot.healthy()` (it previously discarded it and armed only on `drops > 0`, so an all-load-error boot gave 0/0, never armed, and stayed **dead all session**). **Review caught 2 defects the FIX ITSELF introduced:** (1) a legitimately-empty screener was judged unhealthy ⇒ suppressed ⇒ engine kept emitting against YESTERDAY's movers — a money regression; (2) retained entries appended behind successes, but `SignalEngine:903` iterates `loaded` in order and `RiskService:114-118` checks a SHARED per-book max-open slot, so the same registry could trade B after a retry but A after a clean reload. `wouldRegressTheEngine` was ultimately DELETED — per-strategy retention subsumed it. Known asymmetry recorded: `loadErrors` counts transient market-data work AND permanent `StrategyCompiler.compile` alike, so a permanent config error exhausts the chain every CONNECTED (deliberate — a loud wrong-config beats a quiet dead engine). |

PROOF: the Opus review of the D3 reference-data fetcher ran openapi-diff against MAIN's spec and got `GET /api/v1/instruments/indices/{index}/constituents -> Schema: Broken compatibility`, exit 1 — while ci-contracts passed green. WHY IT MATTERS: several consumers read JSON keys OVER THE WIRE with no compile-time link (e.g. strategy-signal's `registry/UniverseResolver:143-152` reads `asOf`/`items`/`exchange`/`tradingsymbol` from market-data's constituents endpoint); a renamed key silently breaks live universe resolution and no compiler catches it — this gate is supposed to be that net. Expect the first run to surface pre-existing incompatibilities accrued while it was blind — report, don't mass-fix; the owner decides. Do NOT block `hotfix/*`. **Prove the fixed gate can FAIL** (rename a response key -> RED; add a field -> green). Found by the Opus cross-vendor review 2026-07-17; verified at source by the Architect. So it was never maskable. ⚠️ **The masking is NOT a property of "structural tests"; it is a property of Spring Modulith's STATIC CACHE of the computed `ApplicationModules`, which makes the retry skip recomputation.** Anything that re-derives its verdict every run (a file scan, a spec re-capture) fails identically on retry and needs no exemption. `ContractCaptureTest` is doubly wrong to add: it recomputes AND its Spring context is a legitimate flake source, so exempting it would convert infrastructure noise into hard build failures. **A POM change was written and then REVERTED** — it would have been config for an impossible case (working principle 2). The durable output is the scope note now in `pom.xml` beside the exemption: *before adding a test here, ask whether it CACHES its verdict across runs in one JVM.* |
| task_06ad72b6 | 2026-07-31 | **Bhavcopy catch-up deadlock — the #1016 fix had the WRONG SCOPE and the deadlock came back.** `BhavcopyBackfillIntegrationTest.aTransientlyMissedDayIsReProbedAndHeals` hit `ERROR: deadlock detected` on ALL THREE surefire attempts on PR #1138's CI shard (a retry loop cannot mask a lock-order problem). `BhavcopyStartupCatchup`'s own javadoc documents this exact deadlock from #1016, fixed then by scoping `artha.bhavcopy.startup-catchup=false` to "tests that manipulate bhavcopy tables" — **the failing test CARRIES that property and deadlocked anyway**, because Spring CACHES test contexts: exactly 1 of the module's 67 IT classes sets it, every other cached context fires the catch-up's fire-and-forget write at boot, and on the 2-core runner a neighbour context is still mid-batch-upsert into `nse_eod_bhavcopy` when any test's whole-table DELETE runs. Hazard = ANY cached-context writer vs ANY test. Fix: the default moves onto `MarketDataIntegrationTestBase`'s `@DynamicPropertySource` (all 67 contexts; outranks `@SpringBootTest` properties, so re-enabling needs deliberate friction), javadoc re-scoped to the true story, and a pin test in a context that does NOT set the property (red-proofed: removing the default flips it to FAILURE). DELIBERATELY no sort-before-upsert — production is single-writer by construction (single-thread executor, D10, no full-table DELETE outside tests); speculative lock-ordering on an unreachable path declined per working principle 2. | **DONE — [#1141](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1141) @ `920a4305`, MERGED 2026-07-31.** 3 review rounds; the review closed a SECOND door the fix had left open — the 19:30-IST eod-cron is 14:00 UTC (prime CI hours), the identical race through the scheduled writer; the substrate now disables BOTH (`startup-catchup=false` + `eod-cron=-`), pinned by a two-door test in a property-silent context, red-proofed. The review also FALSIFIED the fix's own documented opt-out (subclass `@DynamicPropertySource` runs BEFORE the base's, so the base always wins — everything fails silently toward disabled; both docs now say there is NO in-hierarchy opt-out). Test-code + javadoc only; zero production behaviour change — production stays safe by construction (one AtomicBoolean + single-thread executor across startup/cron/manual/refetch, no whole-table DELETE outside tests), confirmed independently by the review. |

| task_6903cd5e | 2026-07-31 | **A FAILED corporate-action event is UNRESUMABLE by construction — the job recovers from a crash but not from an error, even though the error leaves the symbol strictly WORSE off.** `CorporateActionJob.sweepSymbol` resumes only when the latest event status is `BASE_REBUILT`. Its own javadoc calls that checkpoint "the SOLE resume trigger", because after a rebuild the cache equals Kite so detection yields no divergence and no fresh `DETECTED` row — reasoning that applies equally to `FAILED`, which is not checked. The sequence is purge → base re-fetch → chunked cagg refresh, so a failure at the refresh step leaves the base rebuilt and all five caggs purged and never rebuilt, with **nothing that will ever retry**. Measured at filing: **13 symbols** stranded at ~1% of a control's cagg rows (ULTRACEMCO 2,850 `candles_5m` vs INFY 211,783), all with FAILED events on/after 2026-07-21; **0 of 27** control symbols affected. Scoping the sweep via `artha.corporate-actions.symbols` does NOT help — it narrows which symbols are examined, not what the examination concludes. The build must distinguish WHERE the failure occurred (a purge/base-fetch failure is NOT safe to resume refresh-only over incomplete base data) and must bound retries so a permanently-failing symbol does not re-alert nightly. Source: `docs/audits/2026-07-31-e2e-orchestrated-findings.md` §13.5. Distinct from [#1151](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1151), which fixes the decompression-limit CAUSE; this is the durable fix for the unresumable STATE that outlives it. Today's 13 are being repaired operationally by flipping their latest event to `BASE_REBUILT` so the existing resume path picks them up | **DONE [#1156](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1156) @ 72fd45c8** — failure-STAGE-aware resumable state (marketdata V051 `ca_refresh_retry_state`: refresh-stage failures resume, purge/base-fetch failures do NOT; bounded `refresh_attempts` + errdetail capture so a permanently-failing symbol cannot re-alert nightly); sweep silent-failure audit at [#1162](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1162) @ 1fac6f1b; deployed 2026-08-01; the 13 repaired operationally same night (`UPDATE 13`) |

## 6. Deferred-by-design — build only when a consumer appears

Provenance rows live in [`docs/DEFERRED_BACKLOG.md`](../../DEFERRED_BACKLOG.md) (cross-cutting + parked tables):

- ~~`candles_1h` IST re-anchor~~ **DONE (#513, 2026-07-04):** V029 drops+recreates `candles_1h` with
  `time_bucket('1 hour', bucket, 'Asia/Kolkata')` (was UTC-hour, unlike its IST 1d/1w siblings), `WITH

- **OPEN (owner "add to pending, take it later") — FIFO vs RS-priority slot admission for the RS-gated funnel.**
  → **row F3 `fifo-slot-probe`, DONE — #751 @ 9c8df0b6, MERGED + DEPLOYED 2026-07-12** (pointer added by prose-audit
  shard C, 2026-08-02). The word OPEN above is stale: option **(b)** below (the live 7-slot-cap exceedance measure) is
  what shipped, as V034 measurement-only probe columns. Option **(a)** — the offline `portfolioFifoNet` re-run over the
  persisted dropped-names+ranks — is carried as F3's **own** follow-up inside that row, so read F3, not this paragraph.
  The H4 compare's headline "45 vs 24" was FIFO-**gross** vs RS-priority-**net** (confounded). Like-for-like on
  `rs-turnover` (our live config, H4 run 2026-07-07): FIFO-gross **45.0** > RS-priority-gross **32.1** at equal
  DD (~50%) and Sharpe (~0.96), turnover ~equal (1270 vs 1322 trades) → inferred **FIFO-net ~34–37% >
  RS-priority-net 23.8%**. RS-priority IS a real edge (it crushes FIFO in the non-gated `technical`/`turnover`
  variants, 53 vs 30 gross) but is **redundant with the funnel's RS-rank gate** (F1) — re-sorting freed slots
  by RS just concentrates into the top-RS names for no extra selection edge. **To decide (both cheap,
  reversible, no live change):** (a) add a `portfolioFifoNet` accounting to `ManasAroraBacktestService` +
  re-run → a direct net-vs-net read (clean, analytics-only, outside the parity firewall; the FIFO-net figure
  above is inferred, not measured); (b) measure how often the live 20:05 batch actually exceeds the 7-slot
  cap (if rare, the whole question is low-impact live). If confirmed, move live admission to FIFO within the
  gated pool (or raise `max_positions`) — a HOLD-tier live-admission change. Caveat: literal "FIFO" doesn't
  map to a same-day batch (entries fire together at EOD), so the real live lever is "don't pile into top-RS on
  an over-subscribed day / raise the cap."
- **No buildable code left otherwise;** the one open perf follow-up is the audit LOW "serial/N+1 backtest

  [`#646`](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/646), `8a0e72d8`: during market hours (phase OPEN) the
  rollup now requires a fresh `ticks:last-at` heartbeat (≤5 min) or market-data reads DEGRADED (a lingering
  `kite:session:status` key survived a crash → dead feed read UP forever); quiet off-hours; UNKNOWN when the key is
  absent. Extracted a pure `marketDataStatus()` helper so it's unit-tested without the wall-clock (no Clock injection
  after all). `overall` stays kite-driven; still Redis-key-only (no REST fan-out). The register's OTHER half —
  enumerating backtest/strategy-signal/optimizer in `services[]` — is deferred (the rollup is Redis-key-only by design →
  needs a per-service heartbeat-key protocol). **Ops-hardening batch now FULLY shipped (§9-1/§9-14/§9-22/§9-23 + §9-6/§9-7).**

Remaining survivors = accepted single-owner tradeoffs (`ay reset-db -v` blast radius, plaintext broker creds in
`deploy/openalgo/.env`, host-published dev ports, PHC `$$`-escaping) + a few low robustness gaps (dual symbol-grammar
candle reads #214-class, calendar coverage-from-presence, Black76 degenerate-input guards). **VERIFY the cited lines
before actioning** (frozen 2026-07-02; the 3 moot ones no longer apply).

### 8f. F9/F7 arm-effect audit + config fix (2026-07-10)
Owner asked "what effect will F9/F7/audit-doctrine have on the live app" → verified against the live code (not memory):
- **F9 (`ARTHA_PAPER_RISK_ENABLED`) arm = a narrow SCALPER circuit-breaker, no number moves.** The flag gates ONE thing —
  the heat-cap BLOCK in `RiskService.entryAllowed` (`RiskService.java:146-156`): on the scalper book only (its `heat_cap_pct`
  is the sole seed with `enabled=true`, V023; swing rows inert), when open SPAN margin ≥60% of book equity, NEW paper entries
  stop being opened + a `risk_audit` TRIP row + 1 ntfy. Never resizes, never touches an open position, changes ZERO displayed
  figures (`advised_lots`/`margin_snapshot` are already stamped, advisory, not even surfaced in the FE). Adds a synchronous
  margin HTTP hop to the scalper entry path. The 10%→3% scalper daily-loss tighten is already live, flag-independent.
- **F7 (`ARTHA_GRADUATION_PROMOTION_ENABLED`) arm = effectively NO live effect.** Armed, the 21:00-IST evaluator only writes a
  `strategy_graduations` MARKER row + 1 push when a strategy clears ≥50 closed paper trades / expectancy>0 / Sharpe≥0.5 / DD≤25%.
  It does NOT republish, NOT flip `published_version_id`/`status`, NOT swap any live config, NOT change what trades — the V024
  migration header pins "NEVER arms … the owner decides any real live change", and no signal/paper code reads the marker table.
  No FE consumes `/graduation/promotions`. Worst case of arming = a spurious marker + a phone alert. (Task title "shadow→champion"
  is a misnomer — it promotes nothing.)
- **Audit HIGH/MED doctrine = code changes, not flags; the only lever that moves owner numbers** → correctly held for review:
  H6 (CA-unadjusted screener → candidate lists shift near corporate actions), H8 (cheat-3c mislabel → which setups fire), the
  swing exit-parity batch #128 / M36–M40 (exit timing+price → swing paper P&L). M1 matters only once F9 armed; M17/M18/M28/M31
  = no owner-facing behavior change.
- **Config bug fixed in passing** ([`#653`](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/653)): `application.yml:100` read
  env `ARTHA_PAPER_RISK_PER_TRADE_PCT` but compose/.env pass `ARTHA_PAPER_RISK_PER_TRADE_RISK_PCT` (with `RISK`) — so the
  per-trade sizing knob was non-wireable from `.env` (silently fell back to the 1.0 default). Aligned the placeholder to the
  code-bound property `artha.paper.risk.per-trade-risk-pct` (`PaperService.java:143`) + the compose/.env name. Advisory-only
  (advised_lots), so no behaviour change at the default; the knob is now actually settable. Config-only, packages clean.

status column, so a row whose work shipped elsewhere keeps reading as unstarted forever. That misfired on **03**
(shipped 2026-07-11, dispatched 2026-08-03), **02** and **04** (both shipped in July, both dispatched
2026-08-02) — three of six primary candidates, every one caught only by the builder's STEP 0 premise check.

SQUASH SUBJECT that landed on main says `arch-03`. The notation drifts between PR title and squash subject, so
the §-number is exactly the wrong key. What works: `git log origin/main --oneline -i --grep="trial-metrics"`
(6 hits, includes `8e5793bc`). Then confirm by ARTIFACT, which cannot drift —
`git cat-file -e origin/main:<the file the row would create>`.
